**Novak Djokovic**[^cite_note-9] (born 22 May 1987) is a Serbian professional [tennis](29773.md) player. He has been ranked as the [world No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players#Weeks_at_No._1) in men's singles by the [Association of Tennis Professionals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Tennis_Professionals) (ATP) for a record 428 weeks, finished as the [year-end No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players#Year-end_No._1_players) a record eight times, and has been ranked No. 1 at least once in a year for a record 13 different years. Djokovic has won 101 [ATP Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour)–level singles titles, including a record 24 [majors](2092499.md) (including a record ten [Australian Open](97851.md) titles), a record 40 [Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Masters_Series_records_and_statistics#Title_leaders) titles, a record seven [year-end championships](1209231.md), and an [Olympic gold medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_Summer_Olympics). Djokovic is the only man in tennis history to be the reigning champion of all four majors at once across three different surfaces. In singles, he is the only man to achieve a [triple Career Grand Slam](2092499.md#Career_Grand_Slam), and the first player to complete a [Career Golden Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Masters_Series_records_and_statistics#Career_Golden_Masters), and the only player to accomplish it twice. Djokovic is the only player in singles to have won [all of the Big Titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_Big_Titles_singles_champions#Big_Titles_sweep) over the course of his career.

Djokovic began his professional career in 2003. In 2008, at age 20, he disrupted [Roger Federer](262376.md) and [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal)'s streak of 11 consecutive majors by winning his first major title at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). By 2010, Djokovic had begun to separate himself from the rest of the field and, as a result, the trio of Federer, Nadal, and Djokovic was referred to as the "[Big Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Three_\(tennis\))" among fans and commentators, and [Big Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Four_career_statistics) including [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray), creating a [Golden Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Three_\(tennis\)#Reception_and_legacy) of men's tennis. In [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season), Djokovic ascended to No. 1 for the first time, winning three majors and a then-record five Masters titles while going 10–1 against Nadal and Federer. He remained the most successful player in men's tennis for the rest of the decade. Djokovic had his most successful season in [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season), reaching a record 15 consecutive finals and winning 10 Big Titles while earning 31 victories over top-10 players. His dominant run extended through to the [2016 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he completed the [Career Grand Slam](197638.md#Career_Grand_Slam) and a [non-calendar year Grand Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non_calendar_year_Grand_Slam), becoming the first man since [Rod Laver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Laver) in 1969 to hold all four majors simultaneously and setting a rankings points record of 16,950.

In 2017, Djokovic suffered from an elbow injury that weakened his results until the [2018 Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he won the title while ranked No. 21 in the world. Djokovic then returned to a dominant status, winning 11 more major titles and completing his second and third Career Grand Slams. Due to his opposition to the COVID-19 vaccine mandate, he skipped many tournaments in 2022, notably the [Australian Open](69399815.md) and the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), being [deported from the country in the former case](#Opposition_to_the_COVID-19_vaccine_mandate). One year after the Australian visa controversy, he made a successful comeback to reclaim the [2023 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) trophy, and shortly after claimed the all-time record for most men's singles majors titles. In 2024, he became the only player to complete a career sweep of the Big Titles.

Djokovic was awarded the [Laureus World Sportsman of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laureus_World_Sports_Award_for_Sportsman_of_the_Year) five times in 2012, 2015, 2016, 2019 and 2024 which is tied with Roger Federer for the most of all time. He was named one of *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100)*'s 100 most influential people in the world in 2012 and has been listed by *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* as one of the [world's ten highest-paid athletes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_the_world's_highest-paid_athletes) in 2016.

Representing Serbia, Djokovic led the [national tennis team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_Davis_Cup_team) to the [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup) crown in [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Davis_Cup) and the [ATP Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Cup) crown in [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_ATP_Cup). In singles, he won the gold medal at the [2024 Paris Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2024_Summer_Olympics) and the bronze medal at the [2008 Beijing Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2008_Summer_Olympics). He is a recipient of the [Order of Karađorđe's Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Kara%C4%91or%C4%91e's_Star), [Order of St. Sava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_St._Sava), and the [Order of the Republika Srpska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_the_Republika_Srpska). He has been named the [BTA Best Balkan Athlete of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BTA_Best_Balkan_Athlete_of_the_Year) a record eight times.

Beyond competition, Djokovic was elected as the president of the ATP Player Council in 2016. He stepped down in 2020 to front a new player-only tennis association, the [Professional Tennis Players Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Professional_Tennis_Players_Association) (PTPA), which he co-founded with [Vasek Pospisil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasek_Pospisil), citing the need for players to have more influence on the tour and advocating better prize money structure for lower-ranked players. Djokovic is an active [philanthropist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philanthropist). He is the founder of the Novak Djokovic Foundation, which is committed to supporting children from disadvantaged communities. Djokovic was appointed a [UNICEF Goodwill Ambassador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNICEF_Goodwill_Ambassador) in 2015.

## Early and personal life

Novak Djokovic was born on 22 May 1987 in [Belgrade](55904.md), [SR Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Serbia), [SFR Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia), to Dijana ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_name#Maiden_and_married_names) Žagar) and [Srdjan Djokovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srdjan_Djokovic). He is of paternal Serbian and maternal Croatian descent.[^cite_note-Chap1-10] His two younger brothers, [Marko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marko_%C4%90okovi%C4%87) and [Djordje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djordje_Djokovic), have also played professional tennis.[^cite_note-biography-11]

Djokovic began playing tennis at the age of four,[^cite_note-biography-11] after his parents gave him a mini-racket and a soft foam ball, which his father said became "the most beloved toy in his life".[^cite_note-cutler-2016-12] His parents then sent him to a tennis camp in [Novi Sad](113288.md).[^cite_note-cutler-2016-12] In the summer of 1993, as a six-year-old, he met [Jelena Genčić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jelena_Gen%C4%8Di%C4%87) at a tennis camp she was overseeing at [Mount Kopaonik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kopaonik),[^cite_note-\:1-13]<sup>: 21–26 </sup> where Djokovic's parents ran a fast-food parlour.[^cite_note-cutler-2016-12][^cite_note-spiegel-14][^cite_note-NYTimesintro-15] Genčić worked with Djokovic over the following six years, convincing him to hit his backhand with two hands instead of the single hand used by his idol, [Pete Sampras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sampras).[^cite_note-making1-16][^cite_note-nytimes-17] Djokovic has credited Genčić for "shaping my mind as a human being, but also as a professional".[^cite_note-ausopen-18][^cite_note-reuters-19]

During the [Yugoslav Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoslav_Wars) in the late 1990s, Serbia had to endure [embargoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctions_against_Yugoslavia)[^cite_note-reuters-19] and [NATO bombings](322473.md)[^cite_note-spiegel-14][^cite_note-making1-16] because of the [Kosovo War](16760.md).[^cite_note-biography-11][^cite_note-20] At one point Djokovic had to train inside a disused swimming pool converted into a tennis court.[^cite_note-21] Due to his rapid development, Genčić contacted [Nikola Pilić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Pili%C4%87) and in September 1999, Djokovic moved to the Pilić tennis academy in [Oberschleißheim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oberschlei%C3%9Fheim), Germany, spending four years there.[^cite_note-spiegel-14][^cite_note-22] Pilić made him spend several months serving against a wall to improve his technique, and he had him doing wrist flexibility exercises for a year with a rubber exercise band.[^cite_note-spiegel-14]

His father also took him to train at academies in the United States, Italy, and Germany.[^cite_note-cutler-2016-12] Because of the high cost of traveling and training his father took out high-interest loans to help pay for his son's tennis education, putting Djokovic under immense pressure to deliver.[^cite_note-spiegel-14][^cite_note-making1-16]

He met his future wife, Jelena Ristić, in high school, and began dating her in 2005. The two became engaged in September 2013, and on 10 July 2014 the couple were married on Montenegro's [Sveti Stefan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sveti_Stefan) island, in the [Serbian Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church) of [Saint Stephen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Stephen) ([Serbian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_language): Црква Светог Архиђакона Стефана).[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25] He and Ristić had their first child, a boy, in October 2014.[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27] Their daughter was born in 2017.[^cite_note-28]

Djokovic is a self-described [fan of languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multilingualism), speaking [Serbian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_language), English, French, German, Italian, and Spanish to varying levels of proficiency.[^cite_note-Pack-29][^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31][^cite_note-biography-11]

## Career



Novak Djokovic singles ranking history chart

### 2000s

#### 2001–2003: Juniors

Main article: [Novak Djokovic junior years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novak_Djokovic_junior_years)

In 2001, Djokovic won his first title in a second-category tournament in [Messina](59705.md) on the U14 circuit of the [Tennis Europe Junior Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Europe_Junior_Tour), defeating compatriot [Bojan Božović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bojan_Bo%C5%BEovi%C4%87_\(tennis\)) in the final,[^cite_note-suu-32][^cite_note-33] and his second in [Livorno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livorno),[^cite_note-suu-32] where he beat top seed [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) in the semifinals and second seed [Aljoscha Thron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aljoscha_Thron) in the final.[^cite_note-34] In July, he won the [U14 European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_European_Junior_Championships) in [Sanremo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanremo), defeating [Lukáš Lacko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luk%C3%A1%C5%A1_Lacko) in singles[^cite_note-35] and the doubles with Božović against Russians [Alexandre Krasnoroutskiy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandre_Krasnoroutskiy) and Mikhail Bekker.[^cite_note-36][^cite_note-Raonic-37][^cite_note-\:1-13]<sup>: 76 </sup> He led [Yugoslavia](34244.md) to victory in the European Summer Cup,[^cite_note-suu-32] finishing the year as European champion in singles, doubles, and team events. He also earned a silver medal at the [ITF World Junior Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITF_World_Junior_Championship) U14 team competition for [Yugoslavia](34244.md).[^cite_note-biography-11][^cite_note-38] Djokovic ended 2001 ranked No. 1 on the ETA U14 list, with Andy Murray in second place.[^cite_note-suu-32][^cite_note-\:1-13]<sup>: 81 </sup>

In 2002, now competing in U16 events, Djokovic won two major tournaments in France: the Derby Cadets in *La Baule*, defeating [Gaël Monfils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ga%C3%ABl_Monfils) in the final,[^cite_note-39] and *Le Pontet* in [Avignon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avignon).[^cite_note-40] In September, he won his first ITF title in [Pančevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan%C4%8Devo),[^cite_note-dare-3] winning all matches in straight sets, including over No. 1 seed [David Savić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Savi%C4%87) in the final.[^cite_note-activity-41] In November, Djokovic won the Prince Cup in [Miami](53846.md), defeating Stephen Bass in the final,[^cite_note-activity-41] shortly before competing at the [Junior Orange Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_Orange_Bowl_\(tennis\)), where he reached the third round before losing to [Marcos Baghdatis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcos_Baghdatis).[^cite_note-activity-41]

Overall, Djokovic compiled a 40–11 singles and 23–6 doubles record in juniors, achieving a combined junior world ranking of No. 24 in February 2004.[^cite_note-42] His best junior Grand Slam result was reaching the [semifinals of the 2004 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles).[^cite_note-\:1-13]<sup>: 82 </sup> He also competed at the [2003 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles)[^cite_note-43] and [2003 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles).[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-\:1-13]<sup>: 82 </sup>

#### 2003–2005: Start of professional career

Djokovic played his first professional match in January 2003 at a [Futures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Futures_tournaments) event in Oberschleißheim after receiving a wildcard from [Niki Pilić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niki_Pili%C4%87), narrowly losing to [Alex Rădulescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_R%C4%83dulescu).[^cite_note-ATP_Tour-2][^cite_note-\:1-13]<sup>: 80–81 </sup> He won his first Futures title in [Belgrade](55904.md) later that year and ended 2003 ranked No. 687.[^cite_note-making2-45] In 2004, he recorded his first [ATP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour) win during a [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup) match against [Janis Skroderis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janis_Skroderis),[^cite_note-46] and later claimed his first [Challenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Challenger_Tour) title in [Budapest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest_Challenger_\(May\)) on his 17th birthday, beating [Daniele Bracciali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniele_Bracciali) in the final.[^cite_note-\:2-47] He debuted on the [ATP Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour) main draw at the [Croatia Open Umag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_Open_Umag)[^cite_note-48] and secured his first victory at a tournament in [Bucharest](36877.md),[^cite_note-\:2-47][^cite_note-\:1-13]<sup>: 87 </sup> ending the year ranked No. 186.[^cite_note-making2-45]

Djokovic's [Grand Slam](197638.md) debut came at the [2005 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), followed by his first Slam win at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-\:1-13]<sup>: 89–91 </sup> That year, he also reached the third round at both [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles)[^cite_note-\:1-13]<sup>: 91 </sup> and the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he beat [Gaël Monfils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ga%C3%ABl_Monfils) and [Mario Ančić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_An%C4%8Di%C4%87).[^cite_note-49] He impressed at the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_BNP_Paribas_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) by reaching the third round after defeating [Mariano Puerta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariano_Puerta),[^cite_note-50] ending the year ranked world No. 78 as the youngest player in the top 100.[^cite_note-51]

#### 2006: First ATP titles and major quarterfinal

In April 2006, Djokovic won both of his singles matches at a Davis Cup match against [Great Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Britain_Davis_Cup_team), leading [Serbia and Montenegro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro_Davis_Cup_team) to victory. Following this, his family reportedly had discussions with the [Lawn Tennis Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawn_Tennis_Association) about representing Great Britain.[^cite_note-Hodgkinson-52] Djokovic, then world No. 63, initially dismissed the story, calling it a kind gesture following the tie.[^cite_note-53] In 2009, he confirmed the talks were serious but chose to represent Serbia, stating he was "proud of being a Serbian" and that he "didn't want to spoil that just because another country had better conditions".[^cite_note-54]

At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic, then world No. 63, defeated ninth seed [Fernando González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Gonz%C3%A1lez) en route to his first Grand Slam quarterfinal,[^cite_note-1April-55] where, due to issues with his back, he retired against Rafael Nadal after two sets in their first career meeting, launching their [historic rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djokovic%E2%80%93Nadal_rivalry).[^cite_note-56] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached the fourth round before losing to [Mario Ančić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_An%C4%8Di%C4%87) in five sets.[^cite_note-57] Djokovic claimed his first ATP title at the [Dutch Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Open_\(tennis\)) in [Amersfoort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amersfoort), defeating [Nicolás Massú](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Mass%C3%BA) in the final without dropping a set.[^cite_note-1April-55] He won his second title at the [Moselle Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moselle_Open) in [Metz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metz), beating [Jürgen Melzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%BCrgen_Melzer), and broke into the top 20.[^cite_note-1April-55][^cite_note-58] He also made his first Masters quarterfinal at [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Mutua_Madrile%C3%B1a_Masters_Madrid_%E2%80%93_Singles).[^cite_note-59] He ended the season ranked No. 16, the youngest in the top 20.[^cite_note-60]

#### 2007: First ATP 1000 title and major final, top 3

Djokovic began 2007 by winning the [Adelaide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Next_Generation_Adelaide_International_%E2%80%93_Singles) title, then reached the fourth round of the [2007 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Australian_Open), losing in straight sets to Roger Federer,[^cite_note-1April-55] who would go on to win the tournament.[^cite_note-61] This, along with a strong showing at the [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Wells_Open), where he finished runner-up, propelled him into the top 10.[^cite_note-1April-55] In Miami, he defeated Rafael Nadal in the quarterfinals, Andy Murray in the semifinals, and [Guillermo Cañas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillermo_Ca%C3%B1as) in the final, earning his first Masters title and becoming the youngest male champion in the history of the tournament.[^cite_note-1April-55] He contributed a singles win in [Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_Davis_Cup_team)'s [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup) win against [Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia_Davis_Cup_team).[^cite_note-Georgia-62] On clay, he defeated [Richard Gasquet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Gasquet) in the final of the [Estoril Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estoril_Open_\(tennis\)),[^cite_note-63] and reached the quarterfinals in [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rome_Masters) and [Hamburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamburg_Masters), losing to Nadal and [Carlos Moyá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Moy%C3%A1), respectively.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he made his first major semifinal, falling to Nadal.[^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Wimbledon_Championships), Djokovic won a five-hour quarterfinal over [Marcos Baghdatis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcos_Baghdatis) before retiring in the semifinals against Nadal due to elbow issues.[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69]



Djokovic during his first round match at the [2007 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_US_Open_\(tennis\))

Djokovic's breakthrough continued at the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Canada_Masters), where he defeated world No. 3 [Andy Roddick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Roddick), No. 2 Nadal and No. 1 Federer to claim the title, becoming the first player since [Boris Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Becker) in 1994 to beat the top three-ranked players in a single event. Following this, [Björn Borg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_Borg) remarked that Djokovic was "definitely a contender to win a Grand Slam."[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71] Djokovic would then go on to reach his first major final at the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_US_Open_\(tennis\)), where he had five set points in the first set and two in the second set, but lost them all before losing the match in straight sets to the top-seeded Federer.[^cite_note-72] He won his fifth title of the year at the [Vienna Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna_Open), defeating [Stanislas Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanislas_Wawrinka) in the final,[^cite_note-73] and finishing the year ranked No. 3.[^cite_note-74]

#### 2008: First Major title, Olympic Bronze and ATP Finals title

Main article: [2008 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic began the year at the [Hopman Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hopman_Cup) alongside fellow Serbian [Jelena Janković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jelena_Jankovi%C4%87), where they reached the finals, but ultimately lost to the United States in the decisive mixed doubles rubber.[^cite_note-75] The event also marked his first competitive match against [Serena Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serena_Williams).[^cite_note-76] At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Australian_Open), Djokovic reached the final without dropping a set, defeating Roger Federer in the semifinals,[^cite_note-77] which made him the youngest Open Era player to make the last four at all four majors.[^cite_note-78] He beat unseeded [Jo-Wilfried Tsonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo-Wilfried_Tsonga) in four sets in the final, claiming his first Grand Slam singles title. It was the first Grand Slam title in 3 years not won by Roger Federer or Rafael Nadal.[^cite_note-dte-79]



Djokovic celebrating his first [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) title in [Belgrade](55904.md)

Djokovic then reached the semifinals in [Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Tennis_Championships)[^cite_note-80] and won titles at [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Pacific_Life_Open) and the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)), his ninth and tenth titles, respectively.[^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82] He lost to Nadal in the semifinals of both [Hamburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Hamburg_Masters)[^cite_note-83] and the [2008 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_French_Open).[^cite_note-84] On grass, he fell to Nadal in the [Queen's Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Stella_Artois_Championships_%E2%80%93_Singles) final,[^cite_note-85] and suffered a second-round loss at [Wimbledon](91195.md) to [Marat Safin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marat_Safin), ending a streak of five straight major semifinals.[^cite_note-86] After reaching the quarterfinals at the [Rogers Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Rogers_Cup),[^cite_note-87] he qualified for the finals in [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Western_&_Southern_Financial_Group_Masters), ending Nadal's 32-match win streak in the semis,[^cite_note-88] ultimately, however, losing to [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) in the finals.[^cite_note-89] At the [2008 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics), he earned a bronze medal in the singles event and exited in the first round in doubles with partner [Nenad Zimonjić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nenad_Zimonji%C4%87).[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91] Djokovic rounded off the season by winning his first year-end championship title at the [Tennis Masters Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Tennis_Masters_Cup_%E2%80%93_Singles) in Shanghai in November, beating [Nikolay Davydenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolay_Davydenko) in the final.[^cite_note-92]

#### 2009: Ten finals, five titles

Main article: [2009 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic began the year at the [Brisbane International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brisbane_International), where he was upset in the first round by fellow Pilić academy trainee[^cite_note-93] [Ernests Gulbis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernests_Gulbis).[^cite_note-94] At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Australian_Open), he retired in the quarterfinals against [Andy Roddick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Roddick) due to heat-related illness.[^cite_note-95] After a semifinal loss to [Jo-Wilfried Tsonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo-Wilfried_Tsonga) in Marseille, Djokovic won the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), defeating [David Ferrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ferrer) for his 12th career title.[^cite_note-96] At the [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Wells_Masters), he lost to Roddick in the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-97] He then reached the final of the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Miami_Masters), defeating Roger Federer in the semifinals before losing to [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray).[^cite_note-98] During the clay season, Djokovic reached the finals at both the [Monte Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters) and [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)), losing to Rafael Nadal each time.[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100] He then claimed his second title of the year at the inaugural [Serbia Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_Open), defeating [Łukasz Kubot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%81ukasz_Kubot) in the final.[^cite_note-101] At the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Mutua_Madrile%C3%B1a_Madrid_Open) in May, he lost another close semifinal to Nadal in a record-setting match lasting 4 hours and 3 minutes.[^cite_note-102] He exited the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_French_Open) in the third round to [Philipp Kohlschreiber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philipp_Kohlschreiber).[^cite_note-103] Djokovic reached the final of the [Gerry Weber Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Gerry_Weber_Open) on grass but lost to [Tommy Haas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Haas), and then suffered another defeat to Haas in the quarterfinals of [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-104][^cite_note-105] During the [2009 US Open Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_US_Open_Series), he reached the quarterfinals in [Montreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Rogers_Cup),[^cite_note-106] then made the final in [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Western_&_Southern_Financial_Group_Masters), defeating Nadal in the semifinals before falling to Federer.[^cite_note-107] At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached the semifinals, where he was again defeated by Federer.[^cite_note-108] He capped off the season by winning his only Masters title of the year at the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_BNP_Paribas_Masters) after defeating Nadal in the semifinals, and outlasting [Gaël Monfils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ga%C3%ABl_Monfils) in a decisive set tiebreak in the final.[^cite_note-109]

### 2010s

#### 2010: US Open final & Davis Cup crown

Main article: [2010 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

After playing nearly 100 matches in 2009, Djokovic opted to skip early ATP tournaments in 2010 and began the season at the exhibition [Kooyong Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kooyong_Classic), defeating [Tommy Haas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Haas) but losing to [Fernando Verdasco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Verdasco) and [Bernard Tomic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Tomic).[^cite_note-Ironman-110] At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he fell to [Jo-Wilfried Tsonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo-Wilfried_Tsonga) in the quarterfinals despite being a set away from victory.[^cite_note-111] He then reached the semifinals in [Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_ABN_AMRO_World_Tennis_Tournament) and won the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Dubai_Tennis_Championships), defending an ATP title for the first time.[^cite_note-112] In March, he led Serbia to a 3–2 win over the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Davis_Cup_team) in the [2010 Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Davis_Cup), defeating [Sam Querrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Querrey) and [John Isner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Isner).[^cite_note-113] After early losses in [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_BNP_Paribas_Open) and [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Sony_Ericsson_Open), he split with coach [Todd Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todd_Martin).[^cite_note-114] He reached the semifinals of the [Monte Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters) and the quarterfinals in [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)), both times losing to Verdasco.[^cite_note-115] At the [2010 Serbia Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Serbia_Open), he withdrew while trailing [Filip Krajinović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filip_Krajinovi%C4%87), marking his only loss to a player ranked outside the Top 200.[^cite_note-116] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he lost to [Jürgen Melzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%BCrgen_Melzer) in the quarterfinals after leading by two sets — the only time in his career he lost a major match from that position.[^cite_note-117] He captured his first ATP doubles title at the [Aegon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Aegon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Doubles) with [Jonathan Erlich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Erlich).[^cite_note-118] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he lost in the semifinals to [Tomáš Berdych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1%C5%A1_Berdych).[^cite_note-119] At the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Rogers_Cup), Djokovic reached the semifinals, losing to Federer.[^cite_note-120] He also teamed with Nadal in doubles, marking the first pairing of world No. 1 and No. 2 players since [Jimmy Connors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Connors) and [Arthur Ashe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Ashe) in 1976.[^cite_note-121] At the US Open, Djokovic saved two match points at 4–5 in the fifth set before beating Federer in the semi-finals,[^cite_note-122] but lost to Nadal in the final in four sets.[^cite_note-123]

#### 2011: One of the greatest seasons in history

Main article: [2011 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Novak Djokovic celebrates his 2011 Wimbledon semi-final win over Jo-Wilfried Tsonga. The victory meant that Djokovic successfully clinched the ATP world No. 1 Ranking for the first time in his career on 1 July 2011. He also reached his first-ever Wimbledon final, which he eventually won.

Djokovic celebrates upon defeating [Jo-Wilfried Tsonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo-Wilfried_Tsonga) in the semifinals of the [2011 Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), clinching the [world No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players) ranking for the first time in his career.

Djokovic opened the season by winning the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), defeating Roger Federer in the semifinals and [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) in the final, both in straight sets, to claim his second title in Melbourne.[^cite_note-124] He continued his run by beating Federer in the final of the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Dubai_Tennis_Championships),[^cite_note-125] and at [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_BNP_Paribas_Open) he defeated both Federer and Rafael Nadal, becoming only the third player to beat both in the same event twice.[^cite_note-126] In [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Sony_Ericsson_Open), Djokovic defeated Nadal again to claim back-to-back Masters 1000 titles.[^cite_note-127] After winning the [Serbia Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Serbia_Open), he extended his unbeaten streak with titles in [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Mutua_Madrile%C3%B1a_Madrid_Open) and the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)), defeating Nadal in straight sets in both finals—his first wins over Nadal on clay after nine straight losses.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-reversal-129] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_French_Open), Djokovic reached the semifinals before falling to Federer in four sets—his first loss of the year, ending a 43-match win streak.[^cite_note-130] Five weeks later, Djokovic claimed his first [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Wimbledon_Championships) title, defeating Nadal in the final, and secured the world No. 1 ranking for the first time in his career.[^cite_note-131] Federer served for the match and had two match points in the US Open semi-finals, but Djokovic won in five sets.[^cite_note-132] He then beat Nadal in four sets in the final for his first US Open title and third major in 2011.[^cite_note-133]

#### 2012: Australian Open and year-end titles

Main article: [2012 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic began the season by winning the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), defeating [David Ferrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ferrer) in the quarterfinals and [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) in a five-set semifinal lasting 4 hours and 50 minutes. He faced off against Rafael Nadal in their third consecutive major final meeting after last year's Wimbledon and US Open. Djokovic won in 5 hours and 53 minutes, which was the longest major final and longest match in Australian Open history. Djokovic then lost to [John Isner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Isner) in the semifinals of [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_BNP_Paribas_Open) but retained his [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Sony_Ericsson_Open) title by defeating Murray. Djokovic fell to Nadal in the finals of both [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters) and the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Rome_Masters). At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached his first final at the tournament, defeating Roger Federer in the semis. Attempting to hold all four majors at once, he lost to Nadal in four sets. At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he was again beaten by eventual champion Federer in the semifinals, ceding his no. 1 ranking to Federer.

Djokovic was Serbia's flag bearer at the [2012 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Summer_Olympics) in London. He beat [Jo-Wilfried Tsonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo-Wilfried_Tsonga) to reach the semifinals but lost to Murray and then to [Juan Martín del Potro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Mart%C3%ADn_del_Potro) in the bronze medal match, finishing fourth. He defended his [Rogers Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Open_\(tennis\)) title, dropping just one set, but lost the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati_Open) final to Federer. At the US Open, Djokovic lost in the final in five sets to Murray.[^cite_note-134]

Djokovic then won titles at the [China Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_China_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [Shanghai Rolex Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Shanghai_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), notably saving five championship points against Murray in the latter. Despite losing his opening round at the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_BNP_Paribas_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), he reclaimed the No. 1 spot after Federer withdrew from the tournament. He capped off the season by winning his 2nd [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_ATP_World_Tour_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles) title, beating Federer in the final.[^cite_note-135]

#### 2013: Australian Open and year-end titles

Main article: [2013 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic began his 2013 season at the [2013 Hopman Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Hopman_Cup), representing Serbia with [Ana Ivanovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ana_Ivanovic). He won three of his four singles matches, including a final-round win over [Fernando Verdasco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Verdasco), but Serbia lost the final 1–2 to Spain.[^cite_note-136] At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he defeated [Stan Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Wawrinka) in a memorable fourth-round match lasting over five hours,[^cite_note-137] and went on to beat [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) in the final to win a record third consecutive Australian Open title in the Open Era.[^cite_note-138] Djokovic next helped Serbia take a 2–0 lead over Belgium in the [2013 Davis Cup World Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Davis_Cup_World_Group) first round, with a straight-sets win over [Olivier Rochus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Rochus).[^cite_note-139] He then won the [2013 Dubai Tennis Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Dubai_Tennis_Championships), defeating [Tomáš Berdych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1%C5%A1_Berdych) in the final.[^cite_note-140] At [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Wells_Masters), Djokovic's 22-match winning streak was ended by [Juan Martín del Potro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Mart%C3%ADn_del_Potro) in the semifinals.[^cite_note-141] At the [Miami Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Miami_Masters), he was upset in the fourth round by [Tommy Haas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Haas).[^cite_note-142] In Davis Cup quarterfinals, Djokovic helped Serbia defeat the United States with wins over [John Isner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Isner) and [Sam Querrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Querrey).[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144] He then won the [2013 Monte-Carlo Rolex Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters), defeating Rafael Nadal in straight sets to end Nadal's 46-match winning streak at the event.[^cite_note-145] He suffered early exits at the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Open) and [Rome Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rome_Masters), losing to [Grigor Dimitrov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grigor_Dimitrov) and Berdych, respectively.[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] At the [2013 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_French_Open), Djokovic advanced to the semifinals, defeating Wawrinka, Dimitrov, [Philipp Kohlschreiber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philipp_Kohlschreiber), and Tommy Haas, before losing a dramatic five-set match to Nadal.[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic defeated Del Potro in a historic semifinal lasting 4 hours and 44 minutes,[^cite_note-151] but lost the final to Murray in straight sets. Djokovic was a finalist at the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he lost to Nadal in four sets.[^cite_note-152] He later won the [China Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Open_\(tennis\)) and the [Shanghai Masters](23203399.md), extending his unbeaten streak in Asia to 20 matches.[^cite_note-153] He then claimed the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Masters), defeating [David Ferrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ferrer) in the final.[^cite_note-154] Djokovic ended the year by winning the [2013 ATP World Tour Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_ATP_World_Tour_Finals), beating Nadal in the final.[^cite_note-155] He concluded the season with a 24-match winning streak and later announced that [Boris Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Becker) would join his team as head coach for 2014.

#### 2014: Wimbledon and ATP Finals titles, four ATP 1000 wins

Main article: [2014 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic began the year by winning the [Mubadala World Tennis Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Mubadala_World_Tennis_Championship_%E2%80%93_Singles). At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached the quarterfinals with four straight-set wins before losing to [Stanislas Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanislas_Wawrinka) in five sets, ending his 25-match win streak in Melbourne and 14 consecutive Grand Slam semifinals.[^cite_note-156] He won the [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Wells_Masters), defeating Roger Federer, and the [Miami Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Sony_Open_Tennis_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) by beating Rafael Nadal. A wrist injury affected his [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters) campaign, where he lost to Federer in the semifinals. After recovery, he won the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)) over Nadal and donated his \$500,000 prize to victims of the [2014 Southeast Europe floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Southeast_Europe_floods).[^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_French_Open), Djokovic dropped only two sets en route to the final but lost to Nadal in four sets, his first loss to him after four wins.[^cite_note-159] Djokovic won his second [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Wimbledon_Championships) title by defeating Federer in five sets in the final, reclaiming the world No. 1 ranking.[^cite_note-160] He suffered early exits at the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Rogers_Cup) (to [Jo-Wilfried Tsonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo-Wilfried_Tsonga)) and Cincinnati (to [Tommy Robredo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Robredo)). At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_US_Open_\(tennis\)), he reached the semifinals but lost to [Kei Nishikori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kei_Nishikori).[^cite_note-161] He won a fifth [China Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Open_\(tennis\)) title in six years, and after a semifinal loss to Federer in [Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Shanghai_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), claimed the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_BNP_Paribas_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) without dropping a set.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163] At the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_ATP_World_Tour_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles), he set a record by dropping just nine games in the round-robin stage. By reaching the semifinals, Djokovic secured the year-end No. 1 ranking for the third time, tying Nadal for the fifth most. He won the title after Roger Federer withdrew from the final due to a back injury, which marked the first walkover in a final in the 45-year history of the tournament.[^cite_note-164]

#### 2015: Career-best season: three majors, six Masters, and ATP Finals title

Main article: [2015 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic began the season at the [Qatar Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Qatar_ExxonMobil_Open) in Doha, but lost in the quarterfinals against [Ivo Karlović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivo_Karlovi%C4%87) in three tight sets. He then played at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he made it through the first five rounds without dropping a set. In the semifinals, he faced defending champion [Stan Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Wawrinka), this time prevailing in 5 sets, and then beat [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) in the final to win his 5th Australian Open title and 8th major overall.[^cite_note-165]



Djokovic sliding on a hard court to get a ball back

He next competed at the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Dubai_Tennis_Championships) and lost to Federer in the final.[^cite_note-166] After 2 weeks, Djokovic defeated [John Isner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Isner) and Andy Murray en route to the [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) title, beating Federer in three sets.[^cite_note-167] In [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he defeated David Ferrer and John Isner en route to winning his fifth title, defeating Andy Murray in three sets. Djokovic thus became the first player to complete the [Sunshine double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Open_\(tennis\)#Sunshine_Double) three times.[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169] In April, Djokovic clinched his second [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) by beating Tomáš Berdych in the final,[^cite_note-170] and thus became the first man to win the first three [ATP Masters 1000](30872553.md) titles of the season.[^cite_note-171] Djokovic withdrew from the 2015 Madrid Masters,[^cite_note-172] but won the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), making it 4 out of 4 titles in Masters events entered by Djokovic in the season. This final was notable for being the only clay court final ever contested between Djokovic and Federer.

He continued his good form on clay at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) by reaching the final without dropping a set in the first five rounds, including a quarterfinal clash with Nadal and a five-set semifinal victory over Andy Murray which took two days to complete. However, he lost championship match to [Stan Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Wawrinka) in four sets.[^cite_note-173] Five weeks later, he beat [Kevin Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Anderson_\(tennis\)) in the fourth round in five sets, and then went on to claim his third [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) title with a win over Roger Federer.[^cite_note-174]

Djokovic had the chance to become the first man in history to complete [Career Golden Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour_Masters_1000#Career_Golden_Masters) in [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Western_&_Southern_Open), but he lost the final to Federer (Djokovic would accomplish the feat in later years).[^cite_note-175] At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic reached the final for the sixth time in his career, achieving the feat of reaching all four Grand Slam finals in a single calendar year. In the final, he faced Federer once again, defeating him in four sets to win his third Grand Slam title of the year and his tenth major overall.[^cite_note-176]

He returned to [Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_China_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) in October, winning the title for the sixth time, defeating Nadal in straight sets in the final to bring his overall record at the tournament to 29–0.[^cite_note-177] Djokovic then reached the final of the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_BNP_Paribas_Masters), where he defeated Murray in straight sets, taking his fourth title there and a record sixth ATP Masters tournament in one year.[^cite_note-178] After losing to Federer in the round-robin stage of the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_ATP_World_Tour_Finals), in a rematch final, he beat Federer in straight sets to win his fifth ATP Finals title and becoming the first player to win the Finals four consecutive times.[^cite_note-179]

By the end of the season, Djokovic made a season-record 15 consecutive finals, reaching the championship match of every top-level tournament he played (four in majors, eight in Masters, and the final at the ATP Finals). He won 11 titles including a season-record 10 [Big Titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_Tour_top-level_tournament_singles_champions#Statistics). Djokovic set a season-record of 16,585 for most ranking points accumulated as world No. 1. and had a season-record 31 victories over top-10 players, including a 15–4 winning record against the other members of the Big Four. The 2015 season is Djokovic's most successful season as of 2022, and it is considered one of the greatest seasons in tennis history.[^cite_note-180]

#### 2016: 'Nole Slam', four Masters titles, and ranking points record

Main article: [2016 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)



Djokovic kissing [Coupe des Mousquetaires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coupe_des_Mousquetaires) after winning the [2016 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_French_Open), completing "Nole Slam" and a Career Grand Slam

Djokovic won his 60th career title in [Doha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Qatar_ExxonMobil_Open), defeating Rafael Nadal in 73 minutes. He broke his ATP ranking points record, reaching 16,790. Djokovic then claimed his sixth [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), beating Roger Federer in the semifinals and [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) in the final.[^cite_note-cnn.com.ao-181] After recovering from an eye infection at the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he won his fifth [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) title, beating Nadal and [Milos Raonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milos_Raonic). Djokovic's dominance meant world Nos. 2 and 3 combined points still wouldn't surpass him.[^cite_note-182] On 3 April 2016, Djokovic won the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) without dropping a set, claiming his sixth title and tying [Andre Agassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andre_Agassi)'s record.[^cite_note-Miami2016-183] This was his fourth [Sunshine Double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Open_\(tennis\)#Sunshine_Double), the most in history, and third consecutive. The win made him the all-time ATP prize money leader with \$98.2 million.[^cite_note-184] After an early exit at the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles),[^cite_note-185] Djokovic won the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), beating Murray in the final.[^cite_note-186] The next week at the [Rome Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Murray defeated Djokovic; Djokovic still beat Nadal and [Kei Nishikori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kei_Nishikori) in earlier rounds.[^cite_note-187] Djokovic won the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), defeating Murray in four sets, completing the historic [Nole Slam](197638.md#Non-calendar-year_Grand_Slam).[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189] This made Djokovic the eighth player to achieve a [Career Grand Slam](197638.md#Career_Grand_Slam), third after [Don Budge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Budge) and [Rod Laver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Laver) to hold all four major titles simultaneously, and the first to earn \$100 million in prize money.[^cite_note-190] His ranking points rose to a record 16,950.[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-192] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic's 30-match Grand Slam winning streak ended with a third-round loss to [Sam Querrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Querrey), his earliest Slam exit since the 2009 French Open.[^cite_note-193] In late July, Djokovic returned to form, winning his fourth [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Rogers_Cup_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) title, his 30th Masters overall. At the US Open, he lost the final to Wawrinka in four sets.[^cite_note-194]

#### 2017: Split with the team and injury hiatus

Main article: [2017 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

In January, Djokovic defended his title in [Doha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Qatar_ExxonMobil_Open), defeating world No. 1 [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray). At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he lost in the second round to No. 117 [Denis Istomin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Istomin), marking his first early exit since [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and his first loss at a major to a player outside the top 100.[^cite_note-195] In February and March, he was eliminated before the semifinals by [Nick Kyrgios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Kyrgios) at the [Mexican Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). In April, Djokovic reached the quarterfinals of the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), losing to [David Goffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Goffin). He then split with longtime coach [Marián Vajda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mari%C3%A1n_Vajda), fitness coach [Gebhard Phil-Gritsch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gebhard_Phil-Gritsch), and physiotherapist Miljan Amanović to find a new spark. He reached the semifinals at the [Madrid Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), losing to Rafael Nadal, and was runner-up at the [Rome Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), losing to [Alexander Zverev](39755766.md). On 21 May, Djokovic announced [Andre Agassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andre_Agassi) as his new coach, starting at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he lost in the quarterfinals to [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem). He began the grass season at the [Eastbourne International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Aegon_International_Eastbourne_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), winning the title by beating [Gaël Monfils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ga%C3%ABl_Monfils)—his only tournament win without Vajda until their 2022 split. At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he retired in the quarterfinals against [Tomáš Berdych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1%C5%A1_Berdych) due to an elbow injury.[^cite_note-196] On 26 July, Djokovic announced he would miss the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and the rest of the season to recover from his injury.[^cite_note-197] This ended his streak of 51 consecutive Grand Slam appearances since his debut in 2005.[^cite_note-198]

Djokovic ended the year No. 12, his lowest ranking since 2006.

#### 2018: Surgery, two majors, Career Golden Masters

Main article: [2018 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

In January, Djokovic won the [Kooyong Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kooyong_Classic) exhibition against [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem). At the [2018 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached the fourth round before losing to [Chung Hyeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chung_Hyeon). In late January, he underwent elbow surgery.[^cite_note-199] He returned to practice by early March,[^cite_note-200] and played at [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) a week later, losing in the second round to [Taro Daniel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taro_Daniel). He also lost early at the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) to [Benoît Paire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beno%C3%AEt_Paire).[^cite_note-201] Reuniting with coach [Marián Vajda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mari%C3%A1n_Vajda) at the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), Djokovic defeated [Dušan Lajović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Du%C5%A1an_Lajovi%C4%87) and [Borna Ćorić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borna_%C4%86ori%C4%87) before losing to Dominic Thiem. He said, "After two years finally I can play without pain."[^cite_note-202] Following an early loss at [Barcelona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Barcelona_Open_Banco_Sabadell_%E2%80%93_Singles) to [Martin Kližan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Kli%C5%BEan), he showed improvement at the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) by beating [Kei Nishikori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kei_Nishikori)—his first top 20 win in 10 months—but lost to [Kyle Edmund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyle_Edmund) in the second round.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204] He then participated at the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) but lost to eventual champion Rafael Nadal in the semifinal. After failing to defend his finalist points from 2017, Djokovic's ranking fell to No. 22, his first time being out of the top 20 since October 2006. He then lost to unseeded [Marco Cecchinato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Cecchinato) in the quarterfinals of the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-205]

In the second-longest Wimbledon semifinal to date, Djokovic beat Nadal in five sets played over two days.[^cite_note-206] Djokovic then defeated [Kevin Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Anderson_\(tennis\)) in the final in straight sets to win his fourth Wimbledon title and 13th overall major title, and returned to the top 10 in the rankings.[^cite_note-207] Djokovic then beat top seed Roger Federer in straight sets in the final of the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and thus became the first singles player to complete the Career Golden Masters.[^cite_note-208] At the US Open, Djokovic defeated [Juan Martín del Potro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Mart%C3%ADn_del_Potro) in straight sets, winning his third US Open and 14th major title overall.[^cite_note-209]

Djokovic then participated at the [Shanghai Rolex Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Shanghai_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) and won his 4th title at the tournament and 2nd Masters title of the year, beating [Borna Ćorić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borna_%C4%86ori%C4%87) in the final. He did not drop a set nor have his serve broken during the tournament, and his ranking rose to No. 2. Following Nadal's withdrawal from the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Rolex_Paris_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), Djokovic regained the No. 1 ranking, but lost to unseeded [Karen Khachanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Khachanov) in the final. At the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_ATP_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles), he reached the final stage winning all three of his round robin matches and securing the year-end No. 1 for a fifth time, but was upset by Zverev.

#### 2019: Wimbledon and 7th Australian Open titles

Main article: [2019 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic began 2019 at the [Qatar Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Qatar_ExxonMobil_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), losing in the semifinals to [Roberto Bautista Agut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Bautista_Agut). As the top seed at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he defeated Rafael Nadal in the final to claim his record seventh Australian Open and 15th [major](197638.md#Tournaments) title.[^cite_note-210] He was then upset by [Philipp Kohlschreiber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philipp_Kohlschreiber) at the [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) third round,[^cite_note-211] and lost in the fourth round of the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) to Bautista Agut.[^cite_note-212] On clay, Djokovic reached the quarterfinals of the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), losing to [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev).[^cite_note-213] During the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he celebrated his 250th week at [world number 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players) in the [ATP rankings](13480983.md)[^cite_note-214] and won the title by defeating [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas).[^cite_note-215] At the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached the final after beating [Juan Martín del Potro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Mart%C3%ADn_del_Potro),[^cite_note-216] but lost to Nadal.[^cite_note-217] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic reached the semifinals without dropping a set, becoming the first man to reach 10 consecutive French Open quarterfinals. He lost a marathon five-set match to [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem), ending a 26-match winning streak in majors and his bid for a second 'Nole Slam'.[^cite_note-218] Djokovic defended his title at [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), beating Roger Federer in a record 4-hour 57-minute five-set final. Despite winning fewer points, Djokovic saved two championship points to claim his fifth Wimbledon and 16th major title.[^cite_note-wimbledon2019-219][^cite_note-220] He lost in the semifinals of the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) to eventual champion Medvedev,[^cite_note-221] and retired injured in the fourth round of the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) against [Stan Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Wawrinka).[^cite_note-222] In October, Djokovic won the [Japan Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Rakuten_Japan_Open_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Singles) over [John Millman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Millman)[^cite_note-223] but lost in the quarterfinals of the [Shanghai Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Rolex_Shanghai_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) to Tsitsipas.[^cite_note-224] He ended the season and decade by winning his fifth [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Rolex_Paris_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) title against [Denis Shapovalov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Shapovalov),[^cite_note-225] regarded as the most successful player in men's tennis during the decade.[^cite_note-226]

### 2020s

#### 2020: Australian Open title, 2nd Career Golden Masters

Main article: [2020 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

At the inaugural [2020 ATP Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_ATP_Cup), Djokovic led Serbia to victory with six wins, including over [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) in the semifinals and Rafael Nadal in the final.[^cite_note-227] At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he beat Roger Federer in the semifinals and defeated [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem) in five sets in the final, earning his eighth Australian Open and 17th [Grand Slam](197638.md#Tournaments) title.[^cite_note-228] He regained the world No. 1 ranking[^cite_note-229] and became the first player in the [Open Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era) to win Grand Slams in three different decades. It was also his first comeback win from two sets to one down in a major final.[^cite_note-230] Djokovic then claimed his fifth title at the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), defeating [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas) in the final.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232] In June, during the [Adria Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adria_Tour) he organized, Djokovic tested positive for [COVID-19](63030231.md), along with several other participants, prompting criticism over the event's lack of safety protocols.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235] Djokovic later apologized, saying they believed the tournament met all health guidelines, but acknowledged they "were wrong".[^cite_note-236] He later described the criticism as part of a "witch hunt".[^cite_note-237] Djokovic was disqualified from the US Open during his fourth-round match after taking a ball and hitting it towards the advertising hoarding, unintentionally striking a lineswoman in the process.[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239] Djokovic lost in straight sets in the French Open final to Nadal.[^cite_note-240]

#### 2021: Major titles on all three surfaces

Main article: [2021 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic began the year at the [2021 ATP Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_ATP_Cup), winning both singles matches, but Serbia exited in the group stage.[^cite_note-241] He then won his ninth [Australian Open](97851.md) title and 18th major overall, defeating [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) in the final.[^cite_note-242] On 1 March, Djokovic tied and then surpassed Roger Federer's Open Era record of 310 weeks as world No. 1.[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-no1-244] He suffered early losses at the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte-Carlo_Masters) (to [Dan Evans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Evans_\(tennis\)))[^cite_note-245] and [Serbia Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_Open) (to [Aslan Karatsev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aslan_Karatsev)),[^cite_note-246] and reached the final of the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)), defeating [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas) and [Lorenzo Sonego](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Sonego), before losing to Rafael Nadal.

At the [French Open](147724.md), he beat Nadal in a four-set semifinal and came from two sets down to defeat Tsitsipas in the final.[^cite_note-247] He became the first man in the Open Era to win a major after coming from two sets down twice in the same event,[^cite_note-248] and the first to win all four majors at least twice in the [Open Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era).[^cite_note-DCGS-249][^cite_note-250][^cite_note-DETS-251] At [Wimbledon](91195.md), he earned his 100th grass-court win en route to the final,[^cite_note-252] where he defeated [Matteo Berrettini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Berrettini) to win his sixth title and 20th major, tying Federer and Nadal. He became the second man to win majors on all three surfaces in a single year (a "Surface Slam"),[^cite_note-253] and the fifth in the Open Era to achieve the [Channel Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_Slam) (French Open and Wimbledon in the same year).[^cite_note-254] At the [2020 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Summer_Olympics), Djokovic sought a gold medal, but lost to [Alexander Zverev](39755766.md) in the semifinals and to [Pablo Carreño Busta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo_Carre%C3%B1o_Busta) in the bronze match.[^cite_note-255]

In [mixed doubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_doubles), partnering [Nina Stojanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nina_Stojanovi%C4%87), they reached the semifinals but withdrew from the bronze medal match due to Djokovic's shoulder injury.[^cite_note-256] Djokovic entered the US Open in contention for the [Grand Slam](197638.md), a feat in men's singles tennis achieved only by [Don Budge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Budge) in 1938 and [Rod Laver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Laver) in 1962 and 1969. Djokovic defeated Zverev in five sets in the semifinals. In the final, he "looked subdued throughout as he made a high number of uncharacteristic errors" and lost to Medvedev in straight sets.[^cite_note-257]

#### 2022: Australian Open controversy

See also: [§ Opposition to COVID-19 vaccine mandate](#Opposition_to_COVID-19_vaccine_mandate), and [2022 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic was set to start his 2022 season at the [ATP Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_ATP_Cup) in [Sydney](27862.md) but withdrew.[^cite_note-258] To compete in the [Australian Open](69399815.md), players were required to be vaccinated against [COVID-19](63030231.md) or have a medical exemption. Djokovic received an exemption from [Tennis Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Australia) and the [Department of Health (Victoria)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Health_\(Victoria\)), citing a positive COVID-19 test on 16 December 2021.[^cite_note-259] He was granted a visa on 18 November 2021 and arrived in Melbourne on 5 January, but was detained by the [Australian Border Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Border_Force) for failing to meet entry requirements for unvaccinated travellers.[^cite_note-gre22-260] Djokovic later admitted an error in his travel declaration form, as he had been in Spain shortly before arriving.[^cite_note-261] His visa was cancelled, and he was held in a detention hotel pending appeal.[^cite_note-262] On 10 January, the [Federal Circuit and Family Court of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Circuit_and_Family_Court_of_Australia) ruled in Djokovic's favour, ordering his release and noting that he was denied sufficient time to consult with lawyers and tennis officials before his interview.[^cite_note-263] However, on 14 January, [Alex Hawke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Hawke), the [Minister for Immigration, Citizenship, Migrant Services and Multicultural Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Immigration,_Citizenship,_Migrant_Services_and_Multicultural_Affairs), cancelled Djokovic's visa again under the *[Migration Act 1958](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migration_Act_1958)*, citing concerns for public health and order.[^cite_note-264] The [Federal Court of Australia](4688076.md) upheld the decision, and Djokovic was deported on 16 January.[^cite_note-265]

#### 2022: After Australia

In February, Djokovic returned to the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where vaccination was not required.[^cite_note-266] He lost in the quarterfinals to [Jiří Veselý](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ji%C5%99%C3%AD_Vesel%C3%BD), resulting in the loss of his [world No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players) ranking to [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev). It was the first time since 2004 that a player outside the [Big Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Four_\(tennis\)) held the top spot.[^cite_note-267] Djokovic withdrew from the [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) due to U.S. entry restrictions on unvaccinated foreigners.[^cite_note-268] Despite not playing, he regained No. 1 when Medvedev lost early at Indian Wells.[^cite_note-269] He began his clay season at the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), losing his opening match to [Davidovich Fokina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Davidovich_Fokina).[^cite_note-270] At the [Serbia Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Serbia_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), he reached the final but lost to [Andrey Rublev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Rublev).[^cite_note-271] In May, he was beaten by [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) in the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinals.[^cite_note-272] A week later, he won the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), his sixth title there and a record-extending 38th Masters crown, after defeating [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas) in the final. In the semis, he claimed his 1,000th career win by beating [Casper Ruud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casper_Ruud).[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he advanced to the quarterfinals without dropping a set, but lost to Rafael Nadal in their record 59th match, ending his title defense and again dropping to No. 2.[^cite_note-275] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he earned his 80th win at each major with a first-round victory over [Kwon Soon-woo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kwon_Soon-woo), becoming the first player to do so.[^cite_note-276] He reached a record 32nd Grand Slam final after defeating [Cameron Norrie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Norrie) in the semifinals[^cite_note-277] and beat [Nick Kyrgios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Kyrgios) in four sets to claim his seventh Wimbledon and 21st major title, surpassing Roger Federer and moving one behind Nadal.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] Due to U.S. travel restrictions for unvaccinated foreigners, Djokovic was unable to enter the country and withdrew from the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_US_Open_\(tennis\)).[^cite_note-280]

Djokovic then reached the [Rolex Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Rolex_Paris_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) final as the defending champion, but lost to [Holger Rune](60992715.md), which was the first time he lost a Masters final after winning the first set.[^cite_note-281] At the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_ATP_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles), Djokovic won his record-equaling 6th title, going undefeated in the round-robin stage and beating Casper Ruud in the final.[^cite_note-282] Djokovic ended the year as world No. 5.

#### 2023: Record-breaking 24th major & 7th ATP Finals titles

Main article: [2023 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)



Djokovic at the [2023 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_Open)

Djokovic opened the year with his 92nd title at the [Adelaide International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Adelaide_International_1), saving a championship point to beat [Sebastian Korda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Korda). At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), despite a hamstring issue,[^cite_note-283] he defeated [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas) in the final to claim a record-extending [10th title](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Australian_Open_men's_singles_champions) and reclaim the No. 1 ranking.[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285] He missed the [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_BNP_Paribas_Open) and [2023 Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Miami_Open) due to U.S. entry restrictions.
