During the clay season, he exited early at the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) and [Banja Luka Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Srpska_Open), and lost in the [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) quarterfinals. At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic defeated world No. 1 [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) in the semis and beat [Casper Ruud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casper_Ruud) in the final to win a record-breaking 23rd major, becoming the first man to achieve a [triple Career Grand Slam](197638.md#Career_Grand_Slam).[^cite_note-23majors-286] He also regained the top ranking.[^cite_note-287]

At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic reached his 9th final but lost to Alcaraz in five sets, ending a 34-match winning streak at Wimbledon.[^cite_note-288] Djokovic then won the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), beating Alcaraz in 3 hours and 49 minutes in the longest best-of-three-set ATP final, hailed as one of the greatest matches ever.[^cite_note-289] At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he triumphed over Medvedev in straight sets to win his fourth US Open title and a record-extending 24th men's singles [major](197638.md#Tournaments) title overall.[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291] Djokovic became the oldest US Open men's singles champion in the Open Era.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293]

Djokovic ended his season by winning the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_ATP_Finals), clinching the year-end No.1 ranking for a record eighth time.[^cite_note-294]

#### 2024: Olympic Gold, Career Super Slam, oldest ATP No. 1

Main article: [2024 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)



Djokovic posing with his gold medal following the [men's singles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2024_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) final at the [2024 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Summer_Olympics)

At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic reached the semifinals but lost to world No. 4 [Jannik Sinner](60061043.md) in four sets, ending his 33-match win streak at the event and marking his first semifinal defeat there. He called it "one of the worst Grand Slam matches I've ever played".[^cite_note-295] Nonetheless, he equaled Roger Federer's record of 58 Grand Slam singles quarterfinals[^cite_note-296] and retained his world No. 1 ranking. At [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_BNP_Paribas_Open), he lost in the third round to [Luca Nardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luca_Nardi), ranked No. 123—the lowest-ranked player to defeat him at a Masters 1000 or Grand Slam.[^cite_note-297] At the [2024 Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Monte-Carlo_Masters), he reached the semifinals but lost to [Casper Ruud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casper_Ruud). In [Rome](25458.md), after being accidentally struck by a water bottle post-match,[^cite_note-298] he lost in the third round to [Alejandro Tabilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Tabilo). At the [2024 Geneva Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Geneva_Open), he earned his 1,100th career win on his 37th birthday, becoming the third man in the [Open Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era) to reach that mark, and holding the highest win percentage among them (83.5%).[^cite_note-299] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic survived a five-set match against [Lorenzo Musetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Musetti) that ended at 3\:07 a.m.—the latest finish in tournament history.[^cite_note-300] In the fourth round, he defeated [Francisco Cerúndolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Cer%C3%BAndolo) in his longest French Open match (4h39m), surpassing Federer's records for most Grand Slam match wins and quarterfinal appearances.[^cite_note-301] However, he suffered a torn [medial meniscus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medial_meniscus) in his right knee during that match and withdrew before the quarterfinals, losing the No. 1 ranking to Sinner.[^cite_note-302] At the [Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic reached his 37th Grand Slam final, aiming to equal Federer's record of eight titles, but lost in straight sets to [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) in a repeat of the 2023 final.[^cite_note-303][^cite_note-304] At the [2024 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Summer_Olympics), Djokovic defeated [Matthew Ebden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Ebden), Rafael Nadal, [Dominik Koepfer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominik_Koepfer), [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas), and Lorenzo Musetti to reach his first Olympic final.[^cite_note-305] He then beat Carlos Alcaraz in straight sets to win the [gold medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_medal), completing a [Career Golden Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Career_Golden_Slam) and [Career Super Slam](2092499.md#Career_Super_Slam).[^cite_note-306]

#### 2025: 100th ATP title and further records broken

Main article: [2025 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)

Djokovic opened the 2025 season at the [Brisbane International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Brisbane_International_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), losing to [Reilly Opelka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reilly_Opelka) in the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-307] His second-round match at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) marked his 430th career major main draw singles match, surpassing Roger Federer's all-time record.[^cite_note-308] He beat [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) in four sets in the quarter finals.[^cite_note-309] Djokovic retired in his semifinal match against [Alexander Zverev](39755766.md) due to a muscle tear after only one set was played.[^cite_note-310]

At [Doha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Qatar_ExxonMobil_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), he lost to [Matteo Berrettini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Berrettini) in the first round. This marked the first time Djokovic lost in any first round since the [2016 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2016_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). Djokovic's next tournament was the [Indian Wells Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he suffered a second-round defeat to lucky loser [Botic van de Zandschulp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botic_van_de_Zandschulp) – the 2nd year in a row where he lost to a lucky loser at the same tournament, following [Luca Nardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luca_Nardi).

At the [2025 Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Miami_Open), Djokovic beat lucky loser [Camilo Ugo Carabelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camilo_Ugo_Carabelli) in the third round to claim his record 411th career main draw win at the Masters 1000 level, surpassing Nadal's previous record of 410.[^cite_note-atptour-2025-311] He lost in the final to [Jakub Menšík](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakub_Men%C5%A1%C3%ADk).

In May 2025, it was announced that Andy Murray would no longer be working as Djokovic's coach, by mutual agreement reached after Djokovic began working with Murray in November 2024.[^cite_note-312] After Murray's departure Djokovic's assistant coach and analyst, [Boris Bošnjaković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Bo%C5%A1njakovi%C4%87) assumed full coaching responsibility for the remainder of the season.[^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314] Djokovic took a last minute entry to the [2025 Geneva Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Geneva_Open), where he defeated [Hubert Hurkacz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Hurkacz) in the final to claim the 100th ATP singles title of his career, becoming the first ever tennis player to win at least one ATP singles title in twenty consecutive seasons, and the 3rd tennis player in the Open Era to have won 100 or more career titles (after [Jimmy Connors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Connors) and Federer).[^cite_note-315] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic reached a record-extending 51st major semifinal, where he lost in straight sets to world No. 1 [Jannik Sinner](60061043.md).[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317] By winning the quarterfinals, he recorded his 101st win at the tournament, the second most after Rafael Nadal. At [Wimbledon](80007709.md), Djokovic was again defeated by Sinner in the semifinal (his record-extending 52nd semifinal), with Djokovic noting that the "age, the wear and tear of the body" meant he was limited in his ability to beat players like Sinner or Alcaraz. It was the first time he failed to make the Wimbledon final since 2017.[^cite_note-318] At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he lost in straight sets in the semi-finals to Alcaraz.[^cite_note-319]

At the inaugural [Hellenic Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Hellenic_Championship), Djokovic defeated [Lorenzo Musetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Musetti) in the final to claim his [101st](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era_tennis_records_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles#All_tournaments) career [ATP Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour)-level title and his [72nd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era_tennis_records_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles#Court_type_totals_2) title won on hard courts, surpassing an all-time record he jointly held with Roger Federer.[^cite_note-320] Aged 38 years and 5 months old, Djokovic became the oldest men's singles champion on the ATP Tour since its establishment in 1990, and the [third-oldest men's singles tour-level champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era_tennis_records_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles#Miscellaneous) in the [Open Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era).[^cite_note-321]

#### 2026: Oldest Australian Open finalist

Main article: [2026 Novak Djokovic tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Novak_Djokovic_tennis_season)



Novak Djokovic at the [2026 US Open](84011000.md).

In the semi-finals of the [2026 Australian Open](81715568.md), Djokovic defeated two-time defending champion [Jannik Sinner](60061043.md) to advance to his 11th final at [Melbourne Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melbourne_Park), becoming the oldest Australian Open finalist at the age of 38.[^cite_note-\:5-322] However, Djokovic's quest for a record-extending 25th major title ended in the final, where he was defeated by [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md). The result allowed Alcaraz to become the youngest male player to achieve a [Career Grand Slam](197638.md#Career_Grand_Slam).[^cite_note-323]

At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he suffered a shock third round exit to [Joao Fonseca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joao_Fonseca).[^cite_note-324][^cite_note-325]

During the [ Wimbledon](83354744.md) quarter-final against [Felix Auger Aliassime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felix_Auger_Aliassime), Djokovic won 3-2 in a grueling match that lasted 5 hours and 15 minutes. This match was tied for the longest of Djokovic's Wimbledon career, equal to his [2018 Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinal against Rafael Nadal, and the longest quarter-final in Wimbledon history.[^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327] He eventually lost to Sinner in the semi-finals in straight sets.

During the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Cincinnati_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic lost in an upset during the round of 64 against [Thiago Agustín Tirante](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiago_Agust%C3%ADn_Tirante).

Djokovic lost during the first round of the [US Open](84011000.md) against [Mariano Navone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariano_Navone) in 5 sets, marking the first time since the [2006 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Australian_Open) that Djokovic lost his opening match of a Grand Slam.[^cite_note-328] As a result, Djokovic dropped out of the top 10 in the ATP rankings for the first time since June 2018.

## Rivalries

### Prominent rivalries

See also: [Big Three (tennis)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Three_\(tennis\)) and [List of tennis rivalries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tennis_rivalries)

Djokovic has a winning record against all of his top contemporaries, including his fellow [Big Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Three_\(tennis\)) counterparts, [Roger Federer](262376.md) and [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal).

#### Rafael Nadal

Main article: [Djokovic–Nadal rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djokovic%E2%80%93Nadal_rivalry)

Djokovic and Rafael Nadal's rivalry is the most prolific in men's tennis in the [Open Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era). The two faced each other 60 times, with Djokovic leading 31–29.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330] Djokovic leads on hard courts 20–7, while Nadal leads on clay 20–9, and they are tied on grass 2–2.[^cite_note-FEDEX_ATP_HEAD_2_HEADs-331]

Djokovic is the only player to defeat Nadal in all four majors. He is also the player with the most victories over Nadal on clay, having defeated him twice at the French Open and all three clay court Masters events, notably at the [2013 Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), where he ended Nadal's run of eight consecutive titles. The two contested the longest major final ever played at the [2012 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where Djokovic won in five sets after 5 hours and 53 minutes.[^cite_note-2012Australia-332][^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334] Other classics they played include the [2009 Madrid Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Mutua_Madrile%C3%B1a_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinal,[^cite_note-335] [2011 Miami Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Sony_Ericsson_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) final,[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] the [2013 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinal,[^cite_note-338] [2018 Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinal,[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340] and the [2021 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinal.[^cite_note-341]

#### Roger Federer

Main article: [Djokovic–Federer rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djokovic%E2%80%93Federer_rivalry)



Djokovic and Federer after their semifinal match at the [2011 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles)

Djokovic and Roger Federer's rivalry is considered to be one of the greatest rivalries in tennis history. They faced each other 50 times, with Djokovic leading 27–23, including 13–6 in finals (not including a [2014 walkover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_ATP_World_Tour_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles) in favor of Djokovic). Djokovic leads on hard courts 20–18 and on grass 3–1, and they are split 4–4 on clay.[^cite_note-342]

Djokovic is the player with the most victories over Federer and the only player to beat Federer multiple times at his most successful major tournaments: four times at the [Australian Open](97851.md), three times at the [US Open](82729.md), three times at the [Year-end Championship](1209231.md), and most notably, three times in the final of [Wimbledon Championships](91195.md). Their last final was at the [2019 Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where Djokovic won in five sets in the longest final in Wimbledon history.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344] Other notable matches they contested are the [2014 Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles_final) and [2015 Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles_final) finals, along with semifinals at the [2010 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), [2011 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), [2011 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), and [2018 Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Rolex_Paris_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles).[^cite_note-Mitchell-345][^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347]

#### Andy Murray

Main article: [Djokovic–Murray rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djokovic%E2%80%93Murray_rivalry)

Djokovic and [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) met 36 times, with Djokovic leading 25–11.[^cite_note-348] Djokovic leads on hard courts 20–8 and 5–1 on clay, while Murray won their two matches on grass. Djokovic and Murray are one of two pairs to have met in each of the four major finals (the other pair being Djokovic and Nadal). The two are almost exactly the same age, with Murray being a week older than Djokovic, so they progressed through the ranks of the junior circuit together, and Murray was the winner of the first match they ever played as teenagers at *Les [Petits As](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petits_As)* in 2001.[^cite_note-suu-32] They were the [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_ATP_World_Tour) and [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_ATP_World_Tour) year-end top two players in the world, with the battle for the 2016 year-end No. 1 only being decided in the final of the [World Tour Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_ATP_World_Tour_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles), which was won by Murray in straight sets.[^cite_note-349]

One of their most notable matches was a three-set thriller at the final of the [2012 Shanghai Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Shanghai_Rolex_Masters), in which Djokovic saved five championship points to win his first Shanghai Masters title and end Murray's 12–0 winning streak at the event.[^cite_note-350] Tennis pundits have classified many more of their matches as instant classics, such as the [2011 Italian Open semifinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), the [2012 Australian Open semifinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), the [2012 US Open final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles_final), the [2015 semifinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [2016 final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) at the French Open, and the [2017 Qatar Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Qatar_ExxonMobil_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) final.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352]

#### Stan Wawrinka

Djokovic and [Stan Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Wawrinka) have met 27 times, with Djokovic leading 21–6. Although this rivalry is lopsided in favor of Djokovic, the two have contested numerous close matches, including four five-setters at the majors.[^cite_note-353] Wawrinka and Djokovic have met in three consecutive Australian Opens – with each match going to five sets – and a five-setter in the US Open. In the [2013 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) fourth round, Djokovic won 12–10 in a fifth set, with the match being considered one of the best ever played;[^cite_note-354] at the [2013 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinals, Djokovic won 6–4 in the fifth set; at the [2014 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) quarterfinals, Wawrinka won 9–7 in the fifth.[^cite_note-Stanimal-355] Wawrinka's win broke Djokovic's run of 14 consecutive major semifinals and ended a 28-match winning streak, and Wawrinka went on to win his first major title at the tournament.[^cite_note-356] Djokovic got revenge the next year at the [2015 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), winning 6–0 in the fifth set.[^cite_note-357]

At the [2015 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) final, Wawrinka defeated Djokovic in four sets to claim his second major title. Later that year, Djokovic beat Wawrinka at the [Cincinnati Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_BNP_Paribas_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles).[^cite_note-wawrinka-358] At the [2016 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Wawrinka beat Djokovic in a major final for a second time.[^cite_note-359]

Despite Djokovic's 21–6 overall record against Wawrinka, Wawrinka leads Djokovic 3–2 in [ATP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Tennis_Professionals) finals, two of which in major finals.[^cite_note-360] During Djokovic's run of 13 major finals from the 2014 Wimbledon Championships through the 2020 Australian Open, his only two losses were to Wawrinka. Contrary to most high-profile rivalries, the pair have also played doubles together.[^cite_note-361]

#### Jo-Wilfried Tsonga

Djokovic and [Jo-Wilfried Tsonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo-Wilfried_Tsonga) met 23 times, with Djokovic leading 17–6.[^cite_note-vstsonga-362] Their first meeting was in the final of the [2008 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Australian_Open), which Djokovic won in four sets to win his first major singles title.[^cite_note-vstsonga-362] Tsonga got revenge in their next meeting at the majors, the [2010 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Australian_Open) quarterfinals, winning in five sets after Djokovic fell ill during the match.[^cite_note-363] Djokovic then won their next match at the [2011 Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Wimbledon_Championships) semifinals to advance to his first final there, claiming the world No. 1 ranking for the first time in the process.[^cite_note-364] They met again in the quarterfinals of the 2012 French Open, which Djokovic won in five sets after over four hours.[^cite_note-365] They then played a further three matches in 2012, in the quarterfinals of the [Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2012_Summer_Olympics), the final of the [China Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_China_Open_\(tennis\)), and in the round robin stage of the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_ATP_World_Tour_Finals), with Djokovic winning all of them in straight sets.[^cite_note-vstsonga-362] Their final major meeting was in the second round of the [2019 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Australian_Open), which Djokovic won in straight sets.[^cite_note-vstsonga-362]

#### Juan Martín del Potro

Djokovic and [Juan Martín del Potro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Mart%C3%ADn_del_Potro) met 20 times, with Djokovic leading 16–4.[^cite_note-366] Djokovic won their first four meetings before back-to-back victories for del Potro at the 2011 Davis Cup and their Bronze medal match at the [2012 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2012_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) in straight sets. Djokovic won the next four matches before he lost to del Potro at the [2013 Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where the Argentine made his second career Masters final. Djokovic got the upper hand on the rivalry once again by winning two of the most important matches between them to date: an epic five-setter at the [2013 Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinals (which was the longest Wimbledon semifinal at the time),[^cite_note-367] and a thrilling three-setter at the [2013 Shanghai Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Shanghai_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) final. Del Potro upset Djokovic in the first round at the [2016 Rio Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2016_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) in [Rio](25936.md) en route to the final. In 2018, Djokovic defeated del Potro in three close sets in the final of the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), which was the first Grand Slam final for del Potro since his [2009 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) victory. They played their last match at the [2019 Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) quarterfinal, which Djokovic won in a dramatic three-setter after saving two match points.

### Other rivalries

#### Daniil Medvedev

Djokovic and [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) have met 15 times, with Djokovic leading 10–5.[^cite_note-vsmedvedev-368][^cite_note-369] They have contested 4 Grand Slam matches, with Djokovic leading 3–1. Their first Grand Slam match came at the [2019 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) 4th round, which Djokovic won in 4 sets. Their next 3 encounters at the Majors came in finals, with Djokovic winning the [2021 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and the [2023 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) finals, and Medvedev winning his first major title at the [2021 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) against Djokovic in the final, also ending Djokovic's quest for a calendar-year Grand Slam.[^cite_note-370] Medvedev replaced Djokovic as the world No. 1 player when he rose to the top ranking for the first time in February 2022.[^cite_note-371] All 3 Grand Slam finals between Djokovic and Medvedev were straight set wins. The second set of the 2023 US Open, which Djokovic eventually won in a tiebreaker after a grueling 104-minute battle, was one of the longest sets in US Open history.[^cite_note-372]

#### Carlos Alcaraz

Djokovic and [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) have met ten times, with both players winning 5 matches each.[^cite_note-373] Despite a 16-year age gap, their rivalry has remained remarkably competitive. Their first meeting was at the [2022 Madrid Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinals, in which Alcaraz prevailed in a deciding set tiebreaker. Their next meeting was in the semifinals of the [2023 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), a highly anticipated showdown promoted by media and the [ATP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Tennis_Professionals) itself.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377] Djokovic won in four sets, with the match competitive until Alcaraz faltered at the start of the third set due to cramps from mental pressure and physical intensity.[^cite_note-378] They would meet again soon after in the [2023 Wimbledon final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles_final), in which Alcaraz defeated Djokovic in an epic five-setter that lasted 4 hours and 42 minutes, ending his hopes for the calendar Grand Slam and his decade-long, record 45-match [Centre Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_Court) win streak.[^cite_note-379] The pair followed with another epic at the [2023 Cincinnati Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) final, with Djokovic prevailing in three tightly contested sets. The match was the longest best-of-three-sets [ATP Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour) final and the longest match in the tournament's history, at 3 hours and 49 minutes, and was immediately heralded as one of the best matches ever.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] Djokovic won from a set down and down a break in the second set, along with saving a championship point in the second-set tiebreaker.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] Djokovic and Alcaraz met twice in the summer of 2024, both in high-profile finals. On July 14, Alcaraz cruised to victory in straight sets against Djokovic in [a rematch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles_final) of the previous year's Wimbledon final. Three weeks later, on August 4, Djokovic defeated Alcaraz in [the Olympics final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2024_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) held at [Roland Garros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_Roland_Garros). Neither player was broken on serve across two long tie-break sets, with Djokovic claiming both to complete the [career Golden Slam](197638.md#Golden_Slam).[^cite_note-384] Journalist [Steve Flink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Flink) called it "the best two-set match I have ever seen",[^cite_note-385] while *[Tennis.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_\(magazine\))* listed it as its ATP match of the year for 2024.[^cite_note-386] Both players met again in the quarterfinals of [2025 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), in which Djokovic won in four sets[^cite_note-387] and in the semifinals of [2025 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), in which Alcaraz won in straight sets.

#### Jannik Sinner

Djokovic and [Jannik Sinner](60061043.md) have met eleven times, with Sinner leading the rivalry 6–5.[^cite_note-388] Their first encounter came in the [2021 Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles). Djokovic won their first three encounters, with Sinner recording his first win in the [2023 ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_ATP_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles). The pair have played some notable matches, including the final of the 2023 ATP Finals, which Djokovic won,[^cite_note-389] the [2024 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinal, which Sinner won in four sets,[^cite_note-390] and the [2024 Shanghai Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Rolex_Shanghai_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) final, which Sinner won in straight sets.[^cite_note-391] They met back to back in the semifinals at the [2025 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [2025 Wimbledon](80007709.md), where Sinner won both times in straight sets.[^cite_note-392] Their next encounter was at the [2026 Australian Open](81715568.md) semi-final, where Djokovic won in five sets, ending his losing streak against Sinner.[^cite_note-\:5-322]

## Player profile

### Playing style

Djokovic is an [aggressive baseline player](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_strategy#Offensive_baseliner).[^cite_note-tennisworld1-393] His groundstrokes from both wings are consistent, deep, and penetrating. His backhand is widely regarded as the greatest two-handed backhand of all time, due to its effectiveness on both sides of the court and its accuracy.[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396] His best shot is his backhand down the line, with great pace and precision. He excels at returning serve in particular, and regularly ranks among the tour leaders in return points, return games, and break points won. His forehand is deemed to be underrated, yet one of the best, due to its versatility.[^cite_note-397] After great technical difficulties during the 2009 season (coinciding with his switch to the [Head](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_\(company\)) racket series), his [serve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serve_\(tennis\)) is one of his major weapons again, winning him many free points; his first serve is typically hit flat, while he prefers to slice and kick his second serves wide.[^cite_note-tennisworld1-393] He has also led the [ATP Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour) in their career "Under Pressure Rating" statistic, in part because of his prowess at winning deciding sets.[^cite_note-398]

Djokovic has been described as one of the fittest and most complete athletes in sports history, with high agility, court coverage, and mobility, which allows him to hit winners from seemingly indefensible positions.[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400] Because of this, coupled with flexibility and length, he rarely gets [aced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ace_\(tennis\)). [Todd Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todd_Martin), who coached Djokovic between 2009 and 2010, noted that:[^cite_note-401]

> His athleticism is from another world. His return of serve is way better than any other return of serve ever, and I mean way better. Nobody has gotten so many balls back and neutralized so many good serves.

Djokovic's return of serve is a big weapon for him, with which he can be both offensive and defensive. He is highly efficient off both the forehand and backhand return, often getting the return in play deep with pace, neutralizing the advantage the server usually has in a point. [Andre Agassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andre_Agassi) described Djokovic's return of serve as "the precedent-setting standard for the return".[^cite_note-402][^cite_note-403] Occasionally, Djokovic employs a well-disguised backhand underspin [drop shot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drop_shot) and sliced backhand.

Djokovic commented on the modern style of play, including his own, in an interview with [Jim Courier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Courier) after his semifinal win against [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) in the [2012 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Australian_Open) tournament:[^cite_note-404]

> I had a big privilege and honour to meet personally today Mr. [Laver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Laver), and he is one of the biggest, and greatest players ever to play the game, thank you for staying this late, sir, thank you ... even though it would actually be better if we played a couple times serve and volley, but we don't know to play ... we are mostly around here \[points to the area near the baseline], we are running, you know, around the baseline ...

In assessing Djokovic's 2011 season, [Jimmy Connors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Connors) said that Djokovic gives his opponents problems by playing "a little bit old-school, taking the ball earlier, catching the ball on the rise, (and) driving the ball flat." Connors adds that a lot of the topspin that Djokovic's opponents drive at him comes right into his zone, thus his ability to turn defense into offense well.[^cite_note-405]

### Equipment

Entering the pro circuit, Djokovic used the [Head](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_\(company\)) Liquidmetal Radical, but changed sponsors to [Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilson_Sporting_Goods) in 2005. He could not find a Wilson racquet he liked, so Wilson agreed to make him a custom racquet to match his previous one with Head.[^cite_note-406] After the 2008 season, Djokovic re-signed with Head and debuted a new paint job of the Head YouTek Speed Pro at the 2009 [Australian Open](97851.md). He then switched to the Head YouTek IG Speed (18x20) paint job in 2011, and in 2013, he again updated his paint job to the Head Graphene Speed Pro, which included an extensive promotional campaign.[^cite_note-407] Djokovic uses a hybrid of Head Natural Gut (gauge 16) in the mains and Luxilon Big Banger ALU Power Rough (gauge 16L) in the crosses. He also uses Head Synthetic Leather Grip as a replacement grip.[^cite_note-408] In 2012, Djokovic appeared in a television commercial with [Maria Sharapova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Sharapova) promoting the use of Head rackets for various techniques, such as golf and ten-pin bowling.[^cite_note-409]

### Coaching and personal team

See also: [Novak Djokovic coaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novak_Djokovic_coaches)

Djokovic has worked with numerous coaches, trainers, and advisors throughout his career. His earliest mentors were [Jelena Genčić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jelena_Gen%C4%8Di%C4%87) and [Nikola Pilić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Pili%C4%87), whom he credits as foundational influences in his development. Genčić coached him in Belgrade from 1993 to 1999, while Pilić worked with him in Munich from 1999 to 2003.[^cite_note-making1-16][^cite_note-reuters-19] In 2004 and 2005, he was coached by [Dejan Petrović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dejan_Petrovi%C4%87), during which time he rose from outside the top 300 to inside the top 100.[^cite_note-410] From late 2005 to mid-2006, he briefly worked with [Riccardo Piatti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riccardo_Piatti), before parting ways due to scheduling conflicts.[^cite_note-timeline-411][^cite_note-412] From 2006 to 2017, Djokovic's main coach was [Marián Vajda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mari%C3%A1n_Vajda), who became a long-time collaborator. During this period, he also worked occasionally with specialists such as [Mark Woodforde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Woodforde) (2007) and [Todd Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todd_Martin) (2009–2010).[^cite_note-413] Djokovic's physical team included physiotherapist Miljan Amanović, fitness coach Ronen Bega (until 2009), and Austrian trainer Gebhard Phil-Gritsch from 2009 onward.[^cite_note-čovek-414][^cite_note-415] In 2008, he began working with Italian agent Edoardo Artaldi, who, alongside his wife Elena Capellaro, managed Djokovic's affairs and logistics.[^cite_note-chief-416] Their professional relationship lasted until 2023, despite occasional tensions, such as during the final of the [2023 Adelaide International 1 – Men's singles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Adelaide_International_1_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-Artaldi-417] Nutritionist Igor Četojević joined the team in 2010 and helped reform Djokovic's diet, contributing to his physical transformation.[^cite_note-čovek-414][^cite_note-Refrigerator-418] [Boris Bošnjaković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Bo%C5%A1njakovi%C4%87), who had been a regular team member since 2023 took on the head coach job fully in 2025.[^cite_note-419]

## Off the court

### Philanthropy



Kindergarten in [Jalovik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalovik) village, built by the Novak Djokovic Foundation

[^cite_note-420]

In 2007, Djokovic founded the Novak Djokovic Foundation.[^cite_note-421] The organization's mission is to help children from disadvantaged communities grow up and develop in stimulating and safe environments.[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] The foundation partnered with the [World Bank](36752.md) in August 2015 to promote early childhood education in Serbia.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425][^cite_note-426] His foundation has built 50 schools as of April 2022 and are building their 51st, and supported more than 20,800 children and over a thousand families.[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-halfagoal-428]

Djokovic participated in charity matches to raise funds for the reconstruction of the [Avala Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avala_Tower), as well as to aid victims of the [2010 Haiti earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Haiti_earthquake) and the [2010–11 Queensland floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_Queensland_floods).[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431] Starting in 2007, he has established a tradition of hosting and socializing with hundreds of [Kosovo Serb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_Serbs) children during [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup) matches organized in Serbia.[^cite_note-432] Djokovic was selected as the 2012 [Arthur Ashe Humanitarian of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Awards#Arthur_Ashe_Humanitarian_of_the_Year) for his contributions through the foundation, his role as a [UNICEF national ambassador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_UNICEF_Goodwill_Ambassadors#Serbia), and other charitable projects.[^cite_note-433] In August 2015, he was appointed a [UNICEF Goodwill Ambassador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_UNICEF_Goodwill_Ambassadors#International_ambassadors_and_advocates).[^cite_note-UNICEF-434]

During the [2014 Balkans floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Southeast_Europe_floods), Djokovic sparked worldwide financial and media support for victims in [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md), [Croatia](5573.md), and [Serbia](29265.md).[^cite_note-tennis.floods-435] After winning the [2014 Rome Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)), Djokovic donated his prize money to the flood victims in Serbia, while his foundation collected another \$600,000.[^cite_note-tennis.floods-435][^cite_note-halfagoal-428] Following his [2016 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Australian_Open) victory, Djokovic donated \$20,000 to [Melbourne City Mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melbourne_City_Mission)'s early childhood education program to help disadvantaged children.[^cite_note-436] After the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md) spread [to Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Serbia) in March 2020, he and his wife announced that they would donate €1 million for the purchase of ventilators and medical equipment to support hospitals and other medical institutions.[^cite_note-437] He also made a donation to [Bergamo](987207.md), Italy‚ one of the [worst-affected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Italy) Italian provinces, as well as to [Novi Pazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novi_Pazar), Serbia, and [North Mitrovica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Mitrovica), Kosovo.[^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440]

### Sponsorships and business ventures

Djokovic endorses Serbian telecommunications company [Telekom Srbija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telekom_Srbija) and German nutritional supplement brand FitLine.[^cite_note-441]

On turning professional in 2003, Djokovic began wearing [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas) clothing. At the end of 2009, Djokovic signed a 10-year deal with the Italian clothing company [Sergio Tacchini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Tacchini) after Adidas refused to extend his clothing contract (choosing instead to sign [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray)).[^cite_note-442] Tacchini doesn't make shoes, so Djokovic continued with Adidas as his choice of footwear. His sponsorship contract with Tacchini was incentive-heavy, and Djokovic's disproportionate success and dominance in 2011 caused the company to fall behind on bonus payments, leading to the termination of the sponsorship contract.[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444]

From 2011, Djokovic began to wear custom Red and Blue Adidas Barricade 6.0's shoes, referring to the colors of the [Serbian national flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Serbia). By April 2012, the Tacchini deal had fallen first short and then apart. At that point, he was set to join forces with [Nike, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.),[^cite_note-445] but instead, on 23 May 2012, [Uniqlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uniqlo) appointed Djokovic as its global brand ambassador. The five-year sponsorship, reportedly worth €8 million per year,[^cite_note-446] began on 27 May 2012 in Paris' French Open tennis tournament. A year later, Djokovic's long-term footwear deal with Adidas was announced ahead of the 2013 French Open.[^cite_note-447] Since January 2018, Novak has been wearing [Asics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asics) shoes.[^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449]

In August 2011, Djokovic became the brand ambassador of Swiss watch manufacturer [Audemars Piguet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audemars_Piguet).[^cite_note-450] Less than a month later, Djokovic signed a sponsorship deal with German car company [Mercedes-Benz](18564164.md).[^cite_note-451] In March 2012, Djokovic was announced by [Bombardier Aerospace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardier_Aerospace) as its latest [Learjet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Learjet) brand ambassador, thus joining the likes of actor and pilot [John Travolta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Travolta), architect [Frank Gehry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Gehry), maestro [Valery Gergiev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valery_Gergiev), and classical pianist [Lang Lang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lang_Lang).[^cite_note-452] From January 2014, Djokovic has been endorsing French car manufacturer [Peugeot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peugeot).[^cite_note-453] At the same time, he entered into an endorsement deal with Japanese watch manufacturer [Seiko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seiko),[^cite_note-454] having just ended his affiliation with their rivals Audemars Piguet.[^cite_note-455] In early 2015, ahead of the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic teamed up with Australian banking corporation [ANZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_and_New_Zealand_Banking_Group) for a social media campaign to raise money for local communities across the Asia Pacific region.[^cite_note-456][^cite_note-457] At the same time his partnership with [Jacob's Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob's_Creek_\(wine\)), an Australian wine brand owned by [Orlando Wines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlando_Wines), was announced in regards to the production and distribution of 'Made By' film series, a documentary style content meant to "show a side of Novak not seen before as he recounts never before told life stories from Belgrade, Serbia, celebrating what has made him the champion he is today."[^cite_note-458]

Since 2004, the business end of Djokovic's career has been handled by Israeli managers [Amit Naor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amit_Naor) (former pro tennis player turned sports agent) and Allon Khakshouri,[^cite_note-459] a duo which also had [Marat Safin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marat_Safin) and [Dinara Safina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinara_Safina) as its clients. In June 2008, after the duo entered into a partnership with CAA Sports, the sports division of Hollywood talent firm [Creative Artists Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creative_Artists_Agency), meaning that the famous company started representing tennis players for the first time,[^cite_note-460] Djokovic formally signed with CAA Sports.[^cite_note-461] After Djokovic's contract with CAA Sports expired during the summer of 2012, he decided to switch representation, announcing [IMG Worldwide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMG_Worldwide) as his new representatives in December 2012.[^cite_note-462] On 22 May 2017, Djokovic was unveiled as a brand ambassador of [Lacoste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lacoste) after a five-year partnership with [Uniqlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uniqlo).[^cite_note-463]

In April 2021, Djokovic became a brand ambassador for [Raiffeisen Bank International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raiffeisen_Bank_International) and its subsidiaries in Central and Eastern Europe. The bank will help to support Djokovic's tennis academy in [Belgrade](55904.md).[^cite_note-464]

#### Investments

In 2005, as Djokovic moved up the tennis rankings, he began venturing into the business world. Most of his activities in the business arena have been channeled through Family Sport, a legal entity in Serbia established and run by members of his immediate family. Registered as a [limited liability company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limited_liability_company), Family Sport initially focused on [hospitality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hospitality_industry), specifically the restaurant business, by launching Novak Café & Restaurant, a franchise developed on the theme of Djokovic's tennis success. Over time, the company, whose day-to-day operations are mostly handled by Novak's father, Srdjan, and uncle Goran, expanded its activities into real estate, sports/entertainment event organization, and sports apparel distribution.[^cite_note-465]

The company launched Novak Café & Restaurant in 2008 in the Belgrade municipality of [Novi Beograd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novi_Beograd), the flagship location in a [franchised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franchising) chain of theme café-restaurants. During 2009, two more locations were added—one in [Kragujevac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kragujevac) and the other in Belgrade, the city's second, in September at the neighbourhood of [Dorćol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dor%C4%87ol) overlooking the playing courts of [Serbia Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_Open), whose inaugural edition took place several months earlier.[^cite_note-466] On 16 December 2011, a location in [Novi Sad](113288.md) was opened,[^cite_note-467] however, it operated just over three years before closing in late March 2015.[^cite_note-468] [Banja Luka](385109.md) in neighbouring [Bosnia](3463.md) got its Novak Café & Restaurant location on 16 October 2015 within Hotel Trešnja on Banj hill.[^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470]

In 2009, the company bought a [250-series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour_250) ATP tournament known as the [Dutch Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Open_\(tennis\)) and moved it to Serbia, where it was renamed the [Serbia Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_Open). With the help of Belgrade city authorities, the tournament's inaugural edition was held in May 2009 at the city-owned "Milan Gale Muškatirović" courts, located at an attractive spot in the [Dorćol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dor%C4%87ol) neighbourhood.[^cite_note-471] The tournament folded in 2012 after four editions, and its place in the ATP calendar was taken over by the [Düsseldorf Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%BCsseldorf_Open_\(ATP_Tour\)).

In May 2015, right after winning his fourth [Rome Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) title, Djokovic launched a line of nutritional food products called *Djokolife*.[^cite_note-djokolife-472] On 10 April 2016, while in town for the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Monte-Carlo_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), Djokovic opened a vegan restaurant called Eqvita in [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte_Carlo).[^cite_note-473] The restaurant reportedly closed in March 2019.[^cite_note-474]

Djokovic has an 80% stake in biotech firm QuantBioRes, which claims to be developing a drug to treat patients who have contracted [COVID-19](63030231.md).[^cite_note-475] Their research is based on electromagnetic frequency; one biomedical scientist likened it to [homeopathy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeopathy) and argued that it "does not reflect a contemporary understanding of how biochemistry works", while [Peter Collignon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Collignon) commented that their website "describes a way of finding a new molecule without providing any evidence of success."[^cite_note-476]

In August 2025, Djokovic became a co-owner of French Ligue 2 club [Le Mans FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Mans_FC) through the Brazilian consortium OutField, alongside [Formula One](10854.md) drivers [Felipe Massa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felipe_Massa) and [Kevin Magnussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Magnussen), and Georgios Frangulis, CEO of OakBerry.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478] On June 26, 2026, it was announced that Djokovic would join U.S. private equity firm General Atlantic as a Global Strategic Advisor.[^cite_note-479]

### Professional Tennis Players Association

In August 2020, Djokovic resigned from the Players Council of the [Association of Tennis Professionals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Tennis_Professionals) and formed the [Professional Tennis Players Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Professional_Tennis_Players_Association) (PTPA) with [Vasek Pospisil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasek_Pospisil).[^cite_note-480] The pair served as co-presidents of the new organization to promote the interests of male and female tennis players above a ranking of 500 in singles and 200 in doubles,[^cite_note-NYTAug20-481] and wishing to give players more influence on tour.[^cite_note-482]

In January 2026, Djokovic announced that he was stepping away from the PTPA, citing differences over transparency, governance, and his role within the organization.[^cite_note-483]

### In popular culture

Throughout the latter part of the 2007 season, including before Wimbledon and during [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), his comedic impressions of fellow contemporary tennis players received much media play.[^cite_note-484] It began when a [BBC](19344654.md) camera crew recorded some footage of the twenty-year-old impersonating [Maria Sharapova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Sharapova), [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal), [Goran Ivanišević](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goran_Ivani%C5%A1evi%C4%87), and [Lleyton Hewitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lleyton_Hewitt) on a practice court at London's [Queen's Club Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Stella_Artois_Championships_%E2%80%93_Singles) just before Wimbledon.[^cite_note-Bowers2017-Ch7-485] The material — consisting of Djokovic imitating the said players by exaggerating their trademark physical gestures or nervous tics for the entertainment of his coaching team, [Marián Vajda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mari%C3%A1n_Vajda) and [Mark Woodforde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Woodforde) — aired during the BBC's coverage of the tournament and subsequently became popular online.[^cite_note-486] Two months later, at the US Open, a phone video shot by Argentine players of Djokovic doing locker room impressions of players such as [Andy Roddick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Roddick), [Roger Federer](262376.md), [Filippo Volandri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filippo_Volandri), and Rafael Nadal made its way online, becoming viral.[^cite_note-Bowers2017-Ch7-485][^cite_note-487] A few days later, after beating [Carlos Moyá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Moy%C3%A1) in the quarterfinals, [USA Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Network)'s on-court interviewer [Michael Barkann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Barkann) asked Djokovic to perform some impressions, and the player obliged by doing Sharapova and Nadal.[^cite_note-488][^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490]

In addition to Djokovic, the national surge in the popularity of tennis was also inspired by three other up-and-coming young players: twenty-year-old [Ana Ivanovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ana_Ivanovic), twenty-two-year-old [Jelena Janković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jelena_Jankovi%C4%87), and twenty-three-year-old [Janko Tipsarević](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janko_Tipsarevi%C4%87), as evidenced in early December 2007 when a sports-entertainment show named NAJJ Srbije (The Best of Serbia), put together in honour of the four players' respective successes in the 2007 season, drew a capacity crowd to Belgrade's Kombank Arena.[^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492] In May 2008, he was a special guest during the first semifinal of the [Eurovision Song Contest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2008), held in Belgrade that year. He threw a big tennis ball into the crowd, announcing the start of the voting, and together with one of the show's co-presenters, [Željko Joksimović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDeljko_Joksimovi%C4%87), Djokovic sang [Đorđe Marjanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90or%C4%91e_Marjanovi%C4%87)'s song "Beograde".[^cite_note-493]

Throughout late April and early May 2009, during ATP Master Series tournaments in [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Mutua_Madrile%C3%B1a_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), respectively, the Serb was a guest on the *[Fiorello Show](https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiorello%20Show)* on [Sky Uno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Uno) hosted by Italian comedian [Rosario Fiorello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosario_Fiorello)[^cite_note-494] followed by an appearance on [Pablo Motos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo_Motos)' show *[El Hormiguero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Hormiguero)*.[^cite_note-495]

Djokovic is also featured in the music video for the song "[Hello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hello_\(Martin_Solveig_song\))" by [Martin Solveig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Solveig) and [Dragonette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragonette). The video, filmed at [Stade Roland Garros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_Roland_Garros), shows Solveig facing off against [Bob Sinclar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Sinclar), another DJ, in a tennis match. When the referee calls a crucial ball "Out", Djokovic enters the arena and convinces the referee otherwise.[^cite_note-496] In 2010, the Serbian [blues-rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blues-rock) band [Zona B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zona_B) recorded the song "The Joker", dedicating it to Djokovic.[^cite_note-497][^cite_note-498]



Djokovic with [Emir Kusturica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emir_Kusturica) in [Andrićgrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andri%C4%87grad) in January 2014, where he received the [Key to the City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Key_to_the_City)

On 25 June 2011, at the [Serbian National Defense Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_National_Defense_Council)'s seventieth congress in [Chicago](6886.md), Djokovic was unanimously awarded the Order of Serbian National Defense in America I class – the highest decoration of the SND. The order was given to the twenty-four-year-old for his merits on the international sports scene and his contributions to the reputation of Serbs and Serbia around the world.[^cite_note-499] The day after winning his first [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) title and reaching the [No. 1 ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players) for the first time in his career, Djokovic went home to Belgrade for a homecoming celebration in front of the [Serbian National Assembly](619753.md), an event attended by close to 100,000 people.[^cite_note-500]

On 28 November 2011, after returning from London, where he finished early due to failing to progress out of his round-robin group, Djokovic visited his childhood tennis coach [Jelena Genčić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jelena_Gen%C4%8Di%C4%87) at her Belgrade home, bringing the Wimbledon trophy along.[^cite_note-501] The meeting, reportedly their first in more than four years, was recorded by two television crews – a Serbian one shooting for [Aleksandar Gajšek](https://sr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandar%20Gaj%C5%A1ek)'s show *Agape* on [Studio B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTV_Studio_B) television[^cite_note-502][^cite_note-503] and an American one from [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS) television network filming material for Djokovic's upcoming piece on *[60 Minutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/60_Minutes)*. The next day, 29 November 2011, on invitation from film producer [Avi Lerner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avi_Lerner), Djokovic was part of the high-budget Hollywood movie production *[The Expendables 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Expendables_2)* in a cameo playing himself[^cite_note-504] that was shot in a warehouse in the Bulgarian capital of [Sofia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sofia).[^cite_note-505] However, his bit part was later cut out of the final version of the movie.[^cite_note-506]

In March 2012, he was profiled on the CBS show *60 Minutes* by their correspondent [Bob Simon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Simon). He was named among the [100 most influential people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100) of 2012 by *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* magazine.[^cite_note-507]

Djokovic has been a guest on [late-night talk shows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late-night_talk_show), such as *[The Tonight Show with Jay Leno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tonight_Show_with_Jay_Leno)*, *[Veče sa Ivanom Ivanovićem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ve%C4%8De_sa_Ivanom_Ivanovi%C4%87em)*, *[Conan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conan_\(talk_show\))*, *[Late Night with Jimmy Fallon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Night_with_Jimmy_Fallon)*, *[Late Show with David Letterman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Show_with_David_Letterman)*, *[The Late Show with Stephen Colbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Late_Show_with_Stephen_Colbert)*, *[The Jonathan Ross Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jonathan_Ross_Show)* and *[The Ellen DeGeneres Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ellen_DeGeneres_Show)*.[^cite_note-508]

In April 2021, a team of [Balkan](4829.md) biospeleologists named a recently discovered [freshwater snail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freshwater_snail) species, *Travunijana djokovici,* after Djokovic.[^cite_note-509]

In 2022, a book titled *Facing Novak Djokovic*, a compilation of interviews with ATP players who described in detail what it was like to compete against Djokovic, was published.[^cite_note-510]

In 2022, Nikola Vesović, a research associate at the [University of Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Belgrade), announced that a new species of beetle in the genus *[Duvalius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duvalius)* recently discovered near the town of [Ljubovija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ljubovija), Serbia, had been named *Duvalius djokovici* after Djokovic.[^cite_note-511]

Djokovic appears in the 2024 documentary *[Federer: Twelve Final Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federer:_Twelve_Final_Days)* about Roger Federer's final tournament before his retirement, the [2022 Laver Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Laver_Cup). In July 2024, Djokovic became the first man and the first athlete on the cover of *[Vogue Adria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_Adria)*.[^cite_note-512] He appeared on the front cover of the 2024 video game *[Tiebreak: Official game of the ATP and WTA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiebreak:_Official_game_of_the_ATP_and_WTA)*, alongside [Coco Gauff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coco_Gauff). One of the game's features is a "Novak Djokovic Slam Challenge".[^cite_note-513][^cite_note-514]

### Views on diet, medicine, and science

Since 2010, he has been connected with the nutritionist Igor Četojević, who additionally focuses on [Chinese medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_Chinese_medicine) and performs [acupuncture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acupuncture).[^cite_note-čovek-414] He allegedly discovered that Djokovic suffers from [gluten intolerance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gluten_intolerance), using [applied kinesiology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Applied_kinesiology), and that he should not eat [gluten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gluten), removing it from his diet.[^cite_note-Refrigerator-418] He eventually settled on a [vegan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegan) diet, while later sometimes eating [fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish_as_food).[^cite_note-515] He also claims this vegan, plant-based diet cured his persistent allergies and mild asthma.[^cite_note-516] The gluten-free diet has been credited for improving his endurance on the court and playing a role in his subsequent success.[^cite_note-517][^cite_note-518]

Following his elbow surgery in 2018, he stated that he "cried for three days" after it, feeling guilty, because he was "not a fan of surgeries or medications" and wanted "to be as natural as possible". He further stated his belief that human "bodies are self-healing mechanisms".[^cite_note-519][^cite_note-Clarey2020-520]

In his 2013 autobiography, *Serve to Win*, he wrote of a researcher who directed "anger, fear, hostility" at a glass of water, which turned slightly green after a few days, while also directing "love, joy, happiness" at another glass of water, which remained bright and crystal clear in the same period.[^cite_note-Refrigerator-418][^cite_note-521] In 2020, Djokovic spoke of his belief that some people used prayer and gratitude to turn the most toxic food, or polluted water, into healing water. He also stated that scientists \[have] proven that molecules in the water react to our emotions and speech.[^cite_note-522][^cite_note-523] Such claims are scientifically dubious,[^cite_note-524][^cite_note-525] and generally regarded as superstitious beliefs.[^cite_note-FreeChoice-526]

### Opposition to the COVID-19 vaccine mandate

See also: [2022 Australian Open § Djokovic's vaccination and visa controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Australian_Open#Djokovic's_vaccination_and_visa_controversy)

During the ATP Tour's [shutdown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic_on_sports) due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), in a Facebook live stream with other Serbian athletes hosted in April 2020, Djokovic indicated he [opposes vaccination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaccine_hesitancy) and would not be forced to take a COVID vaccine just to be able to return to the Тour.[^cite_note-Clarey2020-520][^cite_note-527] He later clarified his remarks by stating that he is not against all kinds of vaccines, but that he is against forced vaccination.[^cite_note-the_independent-2020-528] He added that he was extremely careful about what he puts into his body.[^cite_note-the_independent-2020-528]

Djokovic's views came under increased scrutiny in late 2021, in the run-up to the [2022 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Australian_Open), after comments made by [Australian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_government) officials indicated that tennis players would need to be vaccinated to enter the tournament.[^cite_note-529] Prior to the tournament, Djokovic had refused to state publicly whether he was vaccinated or not, but had made comments stating his concern over the possibility of a hotel quarantine in Australia.[^cite_note-530] However, while being interviewed by the [Australian Border Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Border_Force) in January 2022, Djokovic confirmed to the officer interviewing him that he was unvaccinated.[^cite_note-531][^cite_note-532]

> "The principles of decision making on my body are more important than any title or anything else."

— Djokovic, on why he is willing to forgo playing major tournaments.

Several commentators felt that Djokovic's stance against the COVID-19 vaccine could damage his placement among the all-time great tennis players, as he would not be able to participate in the major tournaments where vaccination was required for entry[^cite_note-533] while others have applauded him making his own decision.[^cite_note-534][^cite_note-535][^cite_note-536] He was unable to play the [2022 Australian Open](69399815.md), where he was the defending champion and the favorite to win.[^cite_note-22oddsAU-537][^cite_note-tennisconnected.com-538] Shortly thereafter, he lost the No. 1 ranking he had held for a record 373 weeks. Due to the federal government's vaccination policy for non-U.S. citizens, Djokovic was unable to enter the United States to play the [2022 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), another major tournament where he was favored to win.[^cite_note-22oddsUS-539]

In an interview with the [BBC](1139893.md) on 15 February 2022, a few weeks after the tournament, Djokovic stated he does not associate with the wider [anti-vax movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-vaccine_activism). However, he believes in personal freedom of choice and supports an individual's right to choose whether or not they receive a vaccine. He reaffirmed sticking to his principles and his refusal to receive a vaccine, saying that he would be willing to forgo entry into tournaments that are held in countries mandating the vaccine, even if it cost him his career records and placement among the all-time great players.[^cite_note-willing-540]

### Faith and religious beliefs

Djokovic is a member of the [Serbian Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church). He was baptized in May 1992 at the [Žiča Monastery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDi%C4%8Da_Monastery), alongside his mother and younger brother Marko.[^cite_note-541] On 28 April 2011, [Patriarch Irinej of Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriarch_Irinej_of_Serbia) awarded Djokovic the [Order of St. Sava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_St._Sava) I class, the highest decoration of the Serbian Orthodox Church, for his contributions to [monasteries of the Serbian Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medieval_Monuments_in_Kosovo) in [Kosovo](17391.md) and charitable work in Serbia.[^cite_note-spc.rs-542] He has said that he admired and held in high regard [Bishop Amfilohije](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amfilohije,_Metropolitan_of_Montenegro), who played a key part in helping him through a tough time during the [Yugoslav Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoslav_Wars).[^cite_note-543]

Djokovic has been reported to [meditate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_meditation) for up to an hour a day at the Buddhist [Buddhapadipa Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wat_Buddhapadipa) in [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wimbledon,_London), as he appreciates the natural setting and serenity, and is close to monks in the complex.[^cite_note-544] He has spoken of the positive power of meditation.[^cite_note-545][^cite_note-546][^cite_note-547] He is a frequent visitor to the [Bosnian](3463.md) town of [Visoko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visoko) and [its park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_pyramid_claims#Tourism) that is host to several meditation platforms.[^cite_note-548]

### Support of sport and sportspeople

Djokovic is a fan of Serbian football club [Red Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Star_Belgrade),[^cite_note-549] Italian club [Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A.C._Milan),[^cite_note-550] and Portuguese club [Benfica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.L._Benfica),[^cite_note-551] as well as Serbian basketball club [Red Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KK_Crvena_zvezda).[^cite_note-552][^cite_note-553] He has also shown public support for [Croatia at the 2018 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_at_the_2018_FIFA_World_Cup) and when faced with criticism from some within his native country of Serbia, Djokovic replied that "sports have their 'universal language,' they erase boundaries between people, \[and] overcome differences in religion, race and nationality."[^cite_note-554][^cite_note-555][^cite_note-556] Djokovic has expressed admiration for Croatian football player [Luka Modrić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luka_Modri%C4%87), who plays for [Real Madrid](26413.md).[^cite_note-557][^cite_note-558][^cite_note-559] He is a friend of former Serbian tennis player [Ana Ivanovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ana_Ivanovic), whom he has known since the two were children growing up in Serbia.[^cite_note-560] In March 2026 Djokovic attended the World Cup play-off final between the [Bosnia and Herzegovina national football team](1008974.md) versus Italy as a special guest of the [Football Association of Bosnia and Herzegovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_Association_of_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina), where he was greeted with a standing ovation.[^cite_note-561][^cite_note-562] His presence at the match dominated regional media coverage and public discourse, given its historically complex and often turbulent relations.[^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564][^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566][^cite_note-567]

Djokovic is a member of the "Champions for Peace" club, a group of elite athletes committed to serving peace in the world through sport. It was created by Peace and Sport, a Monaco-based international organization.[^cite_note-568]

### Political statements

Following his victory in the first round of the [2023 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_Open), Djokovic wrote "[Kosovo is the heart of Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_is_Serbia). Stop the violence" on the lens of a camera, in response to the recent [clashes in Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_North_Kosovo_crisis#May). The statement was criticized as inappropriate by France's then-[minister of sports](3374119.md) [Amélie Oudéa-Castéra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Am%C3%A9lie_Oud%C3%A9a-Cast%C3%A9ra), with the [Kosovo Olympic Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_Olympic_Committee) asking the [IOC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IOC) to open disciplinary proceedings against the athlete.[^cite_note-569] Djokovic later said he was against any kind of conflict but defended his statement by opining: "Especially as a son of a man born in Kosovo, I feel the need to give my support to our people and to the entirety of Serbia. Kosovo is our cradle, our stronghold, the centre of the most important things for our country. There are many reasons why I wrote that on the camera."[^cite_note-570]

During the [2024–2025 Serbian anti-corruption protests](78597389.md), Djokovic openly supported protesting students and their demands on several occasions.[^cite_note-571][^cite_note-572] Because of this, he immediately received a backlash from the ruling politicians in Serbia.[^cite_note-\:3-573] Former minister [Zorana Mihajlović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zorana_Mihajlovi%C4%87) said that "Djokovic sided with those who commit violence in the country and who want an independent Kosovo",[^cite_note-\:0-574] while government-affiliated [*Informer*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Informer_\(newspaper\)) called him a "failed tennis player",[^cite_note-575] and "a disgrace" who "supports violence".[^cite_note-\:3-573][^cite_note-576] In April 2025, while publicly reading a letter he received from one child mentioning how the child loves watching Đoković, Serbian President [Aleksandar Vučić](13349267.md) declined to read Djokovic's name, instead pronouncing "[Nikola Jokić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Joki%C4%87)".[^cite_note-577]

## Legacy

> "I believe that numbers are numbers and statistics are statistics and, in that sense, I think he \[Djokovic] has better numbers than mine and that is indisputable. It is not beneath me, nor do I have an ego big enough to try and disguise a reality that is not. This is the truth. The rest are tastes, inspiration, sensations that one player or the other may transmit to you, so that you may like one or the other more. I think that with respect to titles, Djokovic is the best in history, and there is nothing to discuss in that."

— [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal) on Djokovic's legacy.

[^cite_note-578]

[^cite_note-579]

[^cite_note-580]

Djokovic is regarded by many observers, tennis players and coaches as the greatest tennis player of all time, primarily for his achievements across all top-level tournaments of the men's professional tour in addition to his time spent with the [world No. 1 ranking](13480983.md#ATP_No._1_ranked_singles_players). Some media outlets, including [Reuters](18998750.md), [Sports Illustrated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Illustrated),[^cite_note-581] [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN), [Marca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marca_\(newspaper\)),[^cite_note-582][^cite_note-583] [Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes),[^cite_note-584] Tennis World USA,[^cite_note-585] [Australian Broadcasting Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Broadcasting_Corporation),[^cite_note-586] and [Sporting News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sporting_News) have named Djokovic the greatest male tennis player in history.[^cite_note-587]

Djokovic has won a record 72 [Big Titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_Tour_top-level_tournament_singles_champions), including an all-time record of 24 [Grand Slam titles](2092499.md), and holds the most [weeks at No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players), the most wins over top 5 and top 10-ranked players, has won all major and [Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Masters_Series_singles_records_and_statistics) events and the [year-end championships](1209231.md) at least twice (which has not been done by another player once). Djokovic also has a winning head-to-head record over his greatest rivals in one of the strongest eras of tennis, which includes multiple victories over them at the majors where they dominated; defeating Federer three times at Wimbledon and Nadal twice at the French Open.[^cite_note-588][^cite_note-Metro-589][^cite_note-sampras-590][^cite_note-591][^cite_note-592][^cite_note-593] In describing how he emerged from the shadow of Federer and Nadal to eventually equal their achievements and then surpass them, Djokovic remarked "2011 was one of the best, if not the best, season I ever had. It kind of changed things around for me. After that, I started to trust myself more in the deciding matches and points. It got me to a champion's mentality. I started to believe I could beat those two guys \[Federer and Nadal], because they were so dominant. They were taking away all the Slams." [^cite_note-594]

Former world No. 1 [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) labelled Djokovic the "greatest tennis player in history" after winning his first major title at the [2021 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) over Djokovic.[^cite_note-595] [Richard Krajicek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Krajicek) and *[The Roar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Roar_\(website\))*, sports opinion website, said that Djokovic should be considered for the greatest player of all time because he is the only one among his rivals who has won all four majors consecutively.[^cite_note-596][^cite_note-597][^cite_note-598] [Patrick Mouratoglou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Mouratoglou) stated, "Novak is the most complete player of all times. That enables him to find the solution to most of the problems on court and this, on every surface. It explains why he is now in the best position to become the GOAT".[^cite_note-599][^cite_note-600]
