Some analysts claim that the [Djokovic–Nadal rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djokovic%E2%80%93Nadal_rivalry) ranks as the best rivalry in tennis history because of the quality of matches they produce.[^cite_note-601][^cite_note-602] [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal) has praised Djokovic's peak level of performance, stating in 2011 (when he went 0–6 against Djokovic for the season) that "\[Djokovic's level is] probably the highest level of tennis that I ever saw."[^cite_note-603] Nadal reiterated this after a one-sided loss in the [2016 Qatar Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Qatar_ExxonMobil_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) final, stating that "I played against a player who did everything perfectly. I don't know anybody who's ever played tennis like this. Since I know this sport I've never seen somebody playing at this level."[^cite_note-604] In 2017, Nadal stated that "at a technical level, when Djokovic has been at the top of his game, I have to say that I've been up against an invincible player."[^cite_note-605][^cite_note-606] Nadal has called Djokovic the greatest tennis player of all time in 2024.[^cite_note-607] [Pat Cash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Cash) emphasized that Djokovic is one of two players who beat Rafael Nadal at the [French Open](147724.md), the other being [Robin Söderling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robin_S%C3%B6derling), which Cash considers to be "the biggest challenge in tennis"; Djokovic has eight victories over Nadal on clay which is more than all other players have collectively won against Nadal on that surface.[^cite_note-Cash-608][^cite_note-609]

In 2023, former world No. 7 [Mardy Fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mardy_Fish) also declared that Djokovic in 2011 was the "best player of all time".[^cite_note-610] In 2023, [Boris Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Becker) compared Djokovic to [Lionel Messi](2150841.md), [Tom Brady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Brady) and [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) in their respective sports, saying that "For me, he is the lion king".[^cite_note-611]

Tennis coach [Nick Bollettieri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Bollettieri) praised Djokovic as "the most complete player ever" in 2011,[^cite_note-612] and "the most perfect player of all time" in 2015:[^cite_note-613]

> When you look at match players in the history of tennis, I don't believe that anybody can equal everything on the court that Djokovic does. I don't think you can find a weakness in his game. His movement, personality, his return of serve, his serve, excellent touch, not hesitant in coming to the net, great serve. Overall, almost every player has a downfall; to me, he doesn't have one. He's perhaps the best put-together player that I've seen over 60 years.[^cite_note-614]

[Andre Agassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andre_Agassi), stated in an interview in 2019 with the Times of India that:

> The highest standard of tennis that I've ever seen is when Novak is playing his best tennis. The single level, for whatever my tennis IQ is worth, is an unmistakable standard to which everybody will strive to be.[^cite_note-615]

[Pete Sampras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sampras), who at the time of his retirement in 2003 was considered by some to be the greatest male tennis player of all time, stated after Djokovic earned a record-breaking seventh year-end No. 1 finish in 2021:

> Seven years for him, I'm sure he sees it as a bonus to all the majors that he's won, but I think he'll appreciate it more as he gets older. He did it at a time where \[sic] he dominated two of the greats, in Roger and Rafa, and he handled the next generation of players very well – all at the same time. I do think what Novak's done over the past 10 years, winning the majors, being consistent, finishing number one for seven years, to me it's a clear sign that he is the greatest of all time.[^cite_note-sampras-590][^cite_note-616]

Some press reports have also called Djokovic one of the greatest athletes of all time.[^cite_note-617][^cite_note-618]

Tennis pundits have classified many of Djokovic's matches as some of the greatest contests ever, such as the [2012 Australian Open final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles_final), in which he beat [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal) in five long and gruelling sets.[^cite_note-2012Australia-332][^cite_note-619] Other matches include the five-set [2011 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinal against [Roger Federer](262376.md),[^cite_note-620][^cite_note-621] the five-set [2013 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) fourth round against [Stan Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Wawrinka),[^cite_note-nguyen-2013-622][^cite_note-tignor-2019-623] the [2018 Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) semifinal against Nadal, which lasted five brutal sets played over two days,[^cite_note-624][^cite_note-625] the five-set [2019 Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) final against Federer, the longest Wimbledon final in history,[^cite_note-626][^cite_note-627] and the [2023 Cincinnati Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) final against [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md), the longest best-of-three-sets final in ATP Tour history.[^cite_note-628]

| 2011–2023 | [Eight-time Year-End world No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players#Year-end_No._1_players) | **Stands alone** |
| 2011–2023 | [Eight-time ITF World Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITF_World_Champion) | **Stands alone** |
| 2007–2023 | 15 times ranked year-end Top 3 | Roger Federer |
| 2007–2025 | 16 times ranked year-end Top 4 | **Stands alone** |
| 2007–2025 | 17 times ranked year-end Top 5 | **Stands alone** |
| 2007–2025 | 18 times ranked year-end Top 10 | Rafael Nadal Roger Federer |
| Other records |  |  |
| 2006–2023 | 61 titles won by defeating multiple top-10 players | **Stands alone** |
| 2009–2021 | Three-peat at 8 different tournaments[^cite_note-Miami2016-183] | **Stands alone** |
| 2010–2023 | 10 winning streaks of 20+ matches | **Stands alone** |
| 2015 | 15 straight finals in a season | **Stands alone** |

| 2018 | All-time prize money leader ([\$194,814,547](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era_tennis_records_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles#Prize_money) as of 2026)[^cite_note-656] | **Stands alone** |
| 2015 | Most prize money won in a season ([\$21,646,145](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era_tennis_records_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles#Prize_money))[^cite_note-657][^cite_note-658] | **Stands alone** |
| 2004–2026 | 84.1% career hardcourt match winning percentage | **Stands alone** |

### Professional awards

- [BTA Best Balkan Athlete of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BTA_Best_Balkan_Athlete_of_the_Year) (8): 2011, 2012, 2013, 2014, 2015, 2019, 2021, 2023.

  [^cite_note-659]

- [ITF World Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITF_World_Champions) (8): 2011, 2012, 2013, 2014, 2015, 2018, 2021, 2023.

- [ATP Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Awards) (8): 2011, 2012, 2014, 2015, 2018, 2020, 2021, 2023.

- [Laureus World Sportsman of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laureus_World_Sports_Award_for_Sportsman_of_the_Year) (5): 2012, 2015, 2016, 2019, 2024.

  [^cite_note-660]

- [BBC Overseas Sports Personality of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Overseas_Sports_Personality_of_the_Year) (1): 2011.

  [^cite_note-661]

- [*L'Équipe* Champion of Champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'%C3%89quipe_Champion_of_Champions) (2): 2021, 2023.

- [Globe Sports Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globe_Soccer_Awards#2025): 2025

### Other achievements

- **Career Golden Slam**: In 2024, by winning the singles gold medal at the [2024 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Summer_Olympics), Djokovic achieved the **Career Golden Slam**, which consists of a tennis player getting the achievement of winning all four majors and the Olympic gold medal during his career.

- **Career Super Slam**: This refers to the achievement of a tennis player winning all four majors, the Olympic gold medal, and the year-end championship throughout a career. Djokovic also secured the **Career Super Slam** by winning the singles gold medal at the [2024 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Summer_Olympics).

- **Channel Slam**: An achievement that refers to the feat of a tennis player winning both the French Open and Wimbledon in a calendar year. Djokovic secured the **Channel Slam&#x20;**&#x20;by winning the aforementioned titles in the 2021 season.

- **Career Grand Slam**: The feat achieved by a tennis player when winning the four majors in either singles, doubles or mixed doubles, throughout his career at least one time each. Djokovic secured the **Career Grand Slam&#x20;**&#x20;when winning the [French Open singles title in the 2016 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).
